  **EXTENDED 2026-08-15, direct request** ("I need Scope 2 to be a seperate wokflow named something appropriate in the Workflow section capsule and then we're able to run it similarly"). Wires Scope #2 into the Electron app's existing "Workflow section"/"Play" mechanism (`app_electron/`) as its own runnable entry, without a deeper `LLMAgent`-level system merge (still explicitly out of scope, unchanged from every prior pass).

  **Read the existing architecture directly before designing anything**: a "capsule" (`components/agent/capsule.py`'s `WorkflowCapsule`) was hardcoded to mean "a `.pt` checkpoint for the same `LLMAgent`" — `app/recorder_bridge.py`'s `run_capsule()` always spawned `python run_task.py --model <checkpoint>`, `isfile()`-gated on that checkpoint existing. Scope #2 has no such checkpoint (its matcher trains fresh, in-process, from `automate.py`'s own stage 4), so it couldn't fit that schema without either breaking the gate or misusing `model_path`.

  **Fix, generic rather than Scope #2-specific** (matches this project's own "don't hardcode for tasks" rule applied at the app layer): `WorkflowCapsule` gained four fields — `kind` ("agent", the original default, or "script"), `entrypoint`, `args`, `cwd` — plus one pure method, `launch_command(repo_root)`, that returns the `(argv, cwd)` to `Popen` for either kind. `run_capsule()` now resolves a capsule **by name** through an **injectable** `CapsuleRegistry` (`Bridge(registry=...)` — same dependency-injection pattern already used for `agent.py`'s `_resolve_control_via_point`'s `uia_mod`/`win32gui_mod`/`user32`), and calls `launch_command()` instead of hardcoding `run_task.py` — the existing, hard-won Popen flags (`stdin=DEVNULL`, `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, unbuffered `-u`, line-by-line stdout pumping into `logs/capsule_activity.log`) are all reused verbatim for both kinds, not duplicated. `tasks/registry.json` gained one new entry, `"Sheet-to-Portal Matcher"` (`kind: "script"`, `entrypoint: components/scope2/automate.py`, `args: --variant v0_base --commit --show --limit 5`) — `--limit 5` mirrors Scope #1's own "one clean record is enough to showcase" bar; `--commit --show` gives real parity with Scope #1's Play (an actual fill+verify+save against the mock portal in a visible browser, not a silent dry run — safe here since the mock portal write is local and disposable, unlike Scope #1's real target app). `route()` (used to pick a checkpoint for `LLMAgent`) is structurally untouched: the new entry's empty `trigger_keywords`/`trigger_apps` mean it can never be returned by `route()`, proven directly in `tests/test_capsule_launch_command.py::TestRouteNeverReturnsAScriptCapsule`, not just assumed from the code shape.

  **Electron side updated to match**: `app_electron/main.js`/`preload.js`'s capsule IPC (`capsule-run`, `capsule-set-current`, `capsule-run-current`) now carries a capsule *name* through the whole chain instead of a bare `model_path` string. `renderer.js`'s Play panel (`loadCapsuleIntoSlot`) hides the Checkpoint dropdown/Deploy button entirely for a `kind="script"` capsule (there's no swappable checkpoint) and instead shows exactly what will run (`entrypoint` + `args`), so clicking Play is never a surprise. The Workflow section (previously only ever listing `data/demos/<group>/` folders scanned off disk) now also renders one card per `kind="script"` registry capsule, unconditionally — even when zero demo groups exist — since Scope #2 has no `data/demos/` group to hang off of at all.

  **`tests/test_recorder_bridge_capsule_run.py` genuinely rewritten**, not just extended — every test's call shape changed from `bridge.run_capsule(<bare checkpoint path>)` to `bridge.run_capsule(<capsule name>)` against an injected, seeded registry, plus a new `TestRunScriptCapsule` class covering the `kind="script"` Popen shape and its own missing-entrypoint error path. New `tests/test_capsule_launch_command.py` (11 tests) covers `launch_command()` directly for both kinds, including path-normalization edge cases caught live on Windows (`os.path.join` doesn't normalize interior `/` in a relative `model_path`, fixed by resolving both sides with `Path(...).resolve()` before comparing in the test, not a code bug). `tests/test_capsule_emoji_field.py` extended with the same backward-compatibility discipline its existing emoji tests use — a registry entry written before these four fields existed still loads cleanly, defaulting `kind` to `"agent"`.

  **Verified directly, not assumed**: ran `python -u components/scope2/automate.py --variant v0_base --commit --limit 5` myself from the repo root — the exact command the new capsule launches (minus `--show`, since a visible browser window isn't needed to confirm the process itself works) — and got a clean 5/5 rows filled and verified, exit code 0, the same staged banner-per-stage output the Activity log will stream live. Full project test suite re-run end to end after all of the above for regressions.

  **One flake investigated, not brushed past**: the first full-suite run also showed 7 errors in `tests/scope2/test_recorder.py`, unrelated on their face to anything this pass touched (that file exercises the coworker's content-script/live-session recorder, not capsules). Confirmed genuinely unrelated rather than assumed: ran that file alone (30 passed, 0 errors) and ran the entire `tests/scope2/` directory together (172 passed, 0 errors) — both clean. Order-dependent flake specific to a ~29-minute single-process full run, not a regression from this change; not investigated further since it doesn't reproduce under any grouping this pass's own changes could plausibly affect.

  **Real gap found live, direct user report** ("Workflow section only shows sheet-to-portal"). Root cause, checked directly, not guessed: `data/demos/` didn't exist in this working copy at all (it's gitignored, and nobody had recorded a demo session through the Electron app's Recorder tab in this checkout — running the car insurance form live via `run_task.py` never touches that folder, it's a separate path). The Workflow section's demo-group cards only ever render from that folder, so `form_filling` had zero cards regardless of this pass's changes — it was only ever reachable indirectly, through a recorded session that happened not to exist. The new script-kind capsule looked fine only because it was deliberately built to not need one. **Fix**: every registered capsule, agent-kind and script-kind alike, now gets its own direct card in the Workflow section (`loadWorkflows()`), unconditionally — not just script-kind ones. Recorded demo-session groups still render as a separate "Recorded sessions" section below when they exist, for browsing actual session history; the two sections are now visually separated with headings rather than conflated.

  **EXTENDED further 2026-08-15, direct user question then request** ("Does Scope #2 have a model to be used?" → "Let's use that"). User pushed back on the Checkpoint dropdown being hidden entirely for Scope #2, correctly sensing there IS a real model somewhere. Checked directly rather than assumed either way: Scope #2's tiny matcher (1,121 params, scores column↔field pairs) is real, but `automate.py` was never wired to a saved checkpoint — it always trains a brand-new, throwaway matcher from scratch on every run, from whichever recorded demonstration (`--session`) it's pointed at. There already IS a real trained artifact sitting in the repo (`components/scope2/data/models/matcher.pt`, ported from the coworker's own repo, `model/matcher.py` already has both `save()`/`load()` ready to go) — just never loaded by anything.

  **Fix, real not cosmetic**: `automate.py` gained an optional `--matcher <path>` flag — when given, it calls `model.matcher.load()` instead of retraining, skipping the training stage entirely. Verified directly, not assumed: ran `automate.py --matcher components/scope2/data/models/matcher.pt --variant v0_base --commit --limit 5` and got byte-identical column matches to the freshly-trained run (YEAR LEVEL→Year 1-5, FINAL GRADE→Grade 0-100, PROGRAM→Course, all confidence 1.00), 5/5 rows filled and verified.

  **One real correctness catch, caught before shipping, not after**: the existing `matcher.pt` in the repo was trained WITHOUT the position-feature mask automate.py always applies at scoring time (`FEATURE_MASK` — the position feature is "measurably harmful across variants," 11/24 with it vs 18/24 without, per the earlier ablation work). Loading that checkpoint as-is while still masking at inference would have been a silent train/inference skew — the model would never have learned to be robust to that feature being zeroed. Fixed by regenerating `matcher.pt` from `automate.py`'s own default session/variant WITH the same mask applied during training too, so the checkpoint and the runtime scoring are now genuinely consistent (1,121 params, same architecture, final training loss 0.0114). The metadata block also now records which features were masked at training time, so this mismatch can be checked directly in the future instead of re-derived from memory.

  **Architecture generalized, not special-cased for Scope #2**: `WorkflowCapsule` gained one more field, `checkpoint_flag` (e.g. `"--matcher"`) — a script-kind capsule can now ALSO declare a real, swappable checkpoint, resolved dynamically from `model_path` at launch time (not baked into the static `args` list), so picking a different checkpoint via the existing Deploy button actually changes what the next Play run loads, not just cosmetic. `CapsuleRegistry.route()` was also hardened at the same time: it now explicitly skips any non-`"agent"`-kind capsule, structurally, rather than relying only on the convention of leaving `trigger_keywords`/`trigger_apps` empty — proven directly with a capsule that has real triggers AND a real `model_path` (exactly Scope #2's new shape) and confirming `route()` still never returns it. The Electron Checkpoint UI's visibility condition was generalized the same way: it now shows whenever a capsule has ANY `model_path`, not just when `kind == "agent"` — Scope #2's capsule gets the real dropdown now, populated from the one real checkpoint file in `components/scope2/data/models/`.

  Full capsule test group re-verified: 46 tests (`test_capsule_emoji_field.py`, `test_capsule_launch_command.py`, `test_recorder_bridge_capsule_run.py`, `test_recorder_bridge_capsule_log_file.py`), including a new end-to-end test proving `checkpoint_flag` reaches the real `Popen` argv through the bridge, not just `launch_command()` in isolation. Full project suite re-run for regressions.

  **EXTENDED further still 2026-08-15, direct request** ("Add a countdown as well to support consistency" — after I explained Scope #1's 5-second pre-run countdown exists because it's driving the user's real mouse/keyboard on the real screen, while Scope #2 drives its own isolated browser and has nothing to click into first). User wanted the same visual treatment anyway, for consistency between the two Play workflows.

  `components/scope2/automate.py` gained its own `print_countdown()`, mirroring `run_task.py`'s exact sentinel format (`COUNTDOWN_BEGIN` / `COUNTDOWN N` / `COUNTDOWN_END`) — the Play panel's `handleCapsuleProgressLine()` already parsed these generically, so the existing countdown widget picked this up with **zero changes needed on the Electron side**, proving out the "genuinely reusable, not `run_task.py`-specific" design noted when this mechanism was first investigated. Also replicated `_flush_safe_print()`'s `OSError` guard from `run_task.py` — this script is spawned through the identical `windowsHide` no-console Electron chain, so it was exposed to the exact same flush-crash risk that fix was originally written for.

  **One real bug caught by actually running it, not assumed from the code**: the informational line printed alongside the countdown ("Starting the matcher — no window to click...") used a real em-dash character, which came out as `�` in the piped subprocess output — a Windows console-encoding mismatch between the child process's default stdout encoding and what a redirected pipe preserves. `run_task.py`'s own countdown message never hit this because its text is plain ASCII. Fixed by switching to a plain `--` (the same ASCII-dash convention already used everywhere else in this codebase's own comments and prose), not by touching Python's stdout encoding configuration — the smaller, better-understood fix.

  **The widget's hint text was hardcoded** to "Click the target window now" — accurate for Scope #1, actively wrong for Scope #2. Rather than leave one workflow's countdown lying to the user, or generalize the wording into something vague enough to fit both, the widget's hint line is now populated dynamically from the line each script prints right after `COUNTDOWN_BEGIN` (still also logged normally in the Activity log below, same as before) — each workflow states its own accurate instruction, with a neutral static fallback ("Get ready…") for any future script that emits the sentinel without one.

  Verified directly: ran `automate.py --matcher ... --commit --limit 5` end to end after both fixes — countdown prints cleanly (no garbled characters), 5/5 rows still filled and verified, identical to every prior run. Full project suite re-run for regressions.

  **EXTENDED 2026-08-17, on `feature/inbox-router`** (checked out to inspect/build on top of the branch-mate's Scope #3 implementation + full visual redesign). Three direct requests handled in sequence:

  1. **"Add a Test section... where you launch the mockups."** Play panel gained a "Test" block below Checkpoint, shown for any loaded task: Task Filler opens the real Car Insurance wx form + Notepad on the intake file (the exact two windows `run_task.py`'s countdown expects already open); Sheet-to-Portal Matcher opens the source spreadsheet + the mock portal's landing page (judgment call — it drives its own browser, nothing to "get ready" the way Scope #1 needs).

  2. **"Add a Settings tab... load LM Studio and use Qwen 2.5-7b Instruct (allow to choose)."** New sidebar tab drives LM Studio's own `lms` CLI (found already installed at `~/.lmstudio/bin/lms.exe`) — server status, a model dropdown built from `lms ls --llm --json` (not hardcoded to one model), start-server and load-model buttons. Verified the CLI's real commands and JSON output shape directly before wiring anything up.

  3. **Real bug found and fixed, direct user report** ("I pressed on the Firefox and it didn't fill it"). `logs/capsule_activity.log` showed the fill and Save both succeeded, then `EOFError` on `input()` — `executor/runner.py`'s `--show` path waits for a real terminal Enter-press before closing the browser, and there is no real terminal when launched via the Electron bridge (`stdin=DEVNULL`, same class of bug already fixed once for `run_task.py`'s countdown). The `finally: browser.close()` fired immediately after, closing the window ~2 seconds after it opened — the user clicked in just after it was already gone.

     Two wrong turns caught before shipping, not assumed away: skipping `browser.close()` alone doesn't work — verified directly that exiting the enclosing `with sync_playwright()` block kills every launched browser regardless. And `sys.stdin.isatty()` as an upfront check is unreliable in this environment — verified directly that it reported `True` even under `stdin=DEVNULL` in a real subprocess chain, which would have silently reintroduced the same crash. The real fix catches the actual `EOFError` and waits (10 minutes, bounded, not forever) inside the still-open `with` block instead of returning early. Verified twice, end to end, spawned exactly the way the bridge spawns it (`stdin=DEVNULL`) — the browser genuinely stays alive afterward. New regression test (`test_show_mode_survives_no_interactive_stdin`) added to `tests/scope2/test_scope2_executor.py`.

  **Second real bug, same evening, direct user report** ("Google Chrome mocksite disappears and reappears"). Root cause, found by reading the code, not guessed: `automate.py` launches **two separate browser instances**, not one — `executor/scanner.py`'s `scan_variants()` (stage 2, gathering field descriptors, functionally required — its output feeds the actual column-matching) launches its own browser and closes it, then `executor/runner.py`'s `run()` (stage 5/6, the fill) launches an entirely different one. The scan browser's default ("old") headless mode can briefly flash a real window on Windows before hiding it, which reads as "the window disappeared and reappeared" even though it's genuinely two separate launches. Fixed by forcing modern headless mode (`headless=True, args=["--headless=new"]`) on the scan browser specifically — the newer mode never creates an OS window at all, so there's nothing left to flash. Verified directly: `scan_variants(["v0_base"])` still returns the correct descriptors with the flag set. (Separately: the "only fills 5 rows" half of the same report wasn't a bug — `--limit 5` in the example command given earlier does exactly what its name says; dropping it fills the full sheet.)

- [x] `scope2_decision_hud` — **Built 2026-09-22, from an open-ended "assess what needs to improve" request.**

  **Assessment first, not assumed.** Compared the three scopes on what a person actually *sees*, not on code quality. Scope #1 has the Electron panel plus a colored countdown; Scope #3 has a full Gmail-clone live UI (`components/inbox_router/local_ui/`, ~50KB) plus colored terminal output, row highlighting, a thinking shimmer and a checkmark burst. Scope #2 had neither: its only user-facing surface was flat grey `print()` and a browser that filled fields silently. So Scope #2 was the scope with the most interesting reasoning — 17 features, Hungarian assignment, threshold induction — and the least visible evidence of it. Its two most defensible claims, that it **abstains** on a decoy column rather than guessing and that it **derives** the pass mark rather than being told it, existed only as grey text in a terminal nobody watches during a defense.

  **Decision 1 — do *not* restyle the mocksite.** The obvious reading of "the Scope #2 UI needs work" is "make the portal prettier". Rejected: `practice_apps/mocksite/shared/styles.css` states its own contract — *"Styling is held constant across every variant so the experiment isolates structure and labelling. Do not fork this file per variant."* Prettifying the instrument would weaken the experiment it exists to run. The portal is untouched; everything added sits outside it.

  **Decision 2 — a runtime overlay in a shadow root, not injected CSS.** New `components/scope2/executor/hud.js` renders the Resolver's decisions into a shadow root, so its styles physically cannot reach the portal and the portal's cannot reach it. It creates no `input`/`select`/`textarea`, so the Page Scanner cannot mistake any of it for a field and the label cascade never sees it; a static test asserts that.

  **Decision 3 — `--show` only.** `executor/runner.py` injects `hud.js` only when `show=True`, so a headless measurement run never loads it. Backed by an end-to-end test asserting `run(show=True)` and `run(show=False)` produce identical rows, identical filled/verified values and identical portal state — if the HUD changed any number the executor reports, it would be contaminating the experiment rather than describing it.

  **Decision 4 — carry abstentions into the executor payload.** `automate.py` was dropping `mapping.abstained` before the executor saw it, so the run log had no record that the Resolver had deliberately refused a column. Refusing a decoy is a result, not a gap, so it now travels with the mapping. `fill_order()` reads only `assignments`/`derived_rules`, so nothing downstream tries to fill them.

  **Two real bugs found while building, both by testing rather than by inspection.**

  1. **Pointer interception**, found by the full suite. With a normal-flow host, Playwright refused to click the portal's own Save button — *"`<div data-agent-hud>` intercepts pointer events"* — and a `--show --commit` run timed out having filled every row correctly. Fixed by making the host zero-area and the entire overlay `pointer-events: none`, with no exception: a HUD that can swallow one of the agent's clicks changes the outcome of the run it is describing. Cost, accepted deliberately: it cannot be mouse-scrolled, so the sections are sized to fit rather than to scroll.

  2. **The panel covered the action**, found by screenshotting it rather than by reading the CSS. The overlay sat on top of the Remarks column and the Save button — Remarks being the rule-derived field, so the demo was hiding the one column it most wanted to show. Fixed by reserving a right-hand gutter on `<html>` instead of floating over the page: applied identically to all eight variants, only under `--show`, and restored on destroy, so the instrument's constancy claim is untouched.

  **Also: colored terminal output for `automate.py`**, matching what Scope #1 and Scope #3 already had — green for an accepted mapping, **yellow (not red) for an abstention**, because abstaining is the system working rather than failing, and blue for the induced rule. Padding is applied *before* coloring: an f-string width counts the invisible ANSI bytes, so coloring first silently breaks column alignment — the same ordering bug `automate_inbox.py`'s own summary already had to fix.

  **Bars animate by `transform`, not by `width`** — flagged by the design hook 2026-09-23 and treated as a real finding rather than a style nit. Animating `width` relayouts on every frame, and **a shadow root is not a layout boundary**: that work lands in the same document the executor is driving, which is precisely the interference `hud.js`'s own header claims it does not cause. Both the confidence bars and the progress bar now grow by `scaleX()`, which runs on the compositor and touches no layout, and the panel carries `contain: layout style` so even reflow inside it stops at its own box. Verified by re-screenshotting: visually identical, mechanically inert. Pinned by `test_bars_animate_by_transform_not_by_width`.

  **Pre-existing test bug fixed in passing.** `tests/scope2/test_architecture_conformance.py` asserted `data/cache` exists, but it is gitignored and created on first use, so the test failed on a fresh clone or any machine where the encoder had not yet run. Verified pre-existing by stashing the working tree and re-running. Now asserts the code still points there instead.

  **Tests** — `tests/scope2/test_hud.py`, 16 tests. Units (payload shape; a mapping missing every optional key; a disabled HUD never touching the page; a throwing HUD swallowed, not raised; the static no-form-controls guard), browser behaviour (shadow-root encapsulation checked with `document.querySelector` rather than a Playwright locator, since locators pierce open shadow roots on purpose and would pass either way; no leaked stylesheet; the abstention and the rule actually rendered; row outline restored exactly; gutter given back on destroy; click-through verified via `elementFromPoint` plus a real Save click), and end-to-end (show/headless result equivalence). Full Scope #2 suite: **189 passing**, up from 182 passing + 3 failing.

- [ ] `scope1_transformer_mode_hud_and_geometry` -- 2026-09-26, reported by the user on Scope #1 Transformer mode: (1) the Agent window's Source and LLM counters stay 0; (2) 'does the screen size and the window size matter? the mouse movement and clicks aren't accurate on my end'.

  (1) FIXED. Read the run log (run_task_20260926_153640): 32 values written by WM_SETTEXT, 16 transformer decisions. With batch fill off, every value is written by the single-field '[OPT2] fast-fill' (edit + combobox) and the checkbox fast-fill -- a source lookup -- and neither path ever reported to the HUD. Only the batch path and the LLM merge paths did. The earlier fix for the same symptom (emit at the decision point) had missed these four sites. DECISION: each reports 'source' through the same once-per-step guard. Test test_every_fast_fill_that_writes_a_value_reports_it pins the RULE (every '[OPT2] fast-fill' log line is followed by a report), not a count; it fails on the previous agent.py.

  (2) INVESTIGATED, NOT CHANGED -- the answer is yes, but not the way it looks. The click itself is exact: the model never outputs a mouse position, it picks one of the on-screen elements (click_elem pointer) and the click goes to that element's UIA box centre (transformer.py predict). The log shows 'SnapClick (838,722) -> (838,722) dist=0 px', and the user's display is 1920x1080 at 100% scaling, so no DPI mismatch. What differs is WHICH element the model picks: every element's box is fed to it divided by the screen resolution (_encode_element: x1/W, y1/H...). All 11,064 training steps were recorded on a 1920x1200 screen with the form window mostly at the right (x~841, y~11, 1075x1025). The live run: 1920x1080 screen, form at x=440 and x=602, 1040x820 -- and TWO form windows open at once. So the model sees positions it never trained on and can point at the wrong field (step 2 pointed at the 'Paperless' checkbox while Policy Status was next).

  OPTIONS OFFERED, none chosen yet: (a) for a demo, close the extra form and place the one form where training had it; (b) the real fix -- encode positions relative to the form window, not the screen, and retrain, so the model no longer depends on where the window sits or how big the screen is.
- [x] `electron_run_views_and_scope2_hud_fix` -- 2026-09-26, reported by the user testing the new Scope #2 from Electron: (1) the floating Agent window showed nothing for Scope #2; (2) after Stop, 'Stopped -- you have control again' was drawn over Home's 'Nothing running / Show Intern the task once'. Both were caused by scope2_source_llm_like_scope1, found here, not before it shipped.

  AGENT WINDOW BLANK -- checked end to end instead of guessed: logs/capsule_activity.log held all ~400 SCOPE2HUD lines and the bridge forwards every line, so the data reached Electron. Replaying those exact lines into agent-hud.html in headless Chromium threw 'ReferenceError: abst is not defined' in s2Init() on the first (init) message: merging refusals into one list removed that variable but one later line still read it. node --check passed because the code is valid syntax; only running the page shows it. Fixed; the replayed window now renders every mapping tagged source/LLM, the refused field with the LLM's reason, and the rule.

  STOPPED OVER HOME, and DURATION '--' -- one root cause. The Running view and the elapsed timer were started only by COUNTDOWN_BEGIN, and Scope #2's countdown had been removed. So for Scope #2 the app never entered its running state, and showFinished() then showed the Tasks summary without leaving whatever section was on screen. DECISION: a run starts at capsule_started, which every task sends -- Running view, timer and agent pill move there; COUNTDOWN_BEGIN keeps only the countdown overlay. Running/Finished now go through showTaskRunView(), which switches to the Tasks section first (only if elsewhere, since showMain() also reloads the task list), so the summary can never overlap Home. And the bridge now emits capsule_started BEFORE it starts pumping output, since that event now resets the activity feed.

  TESTS, both new, both drive the real pages in Chromium: tests/test_agent_hud_page.py (4) feeds lines printed by the executor's own Hud/hud_payload; tests/test_renderer_run_views.py (5) fakes every preload API and replays the bridge's own events -- no-countdown run gets the Running view, Stop shows the summary alone, duration measured, Scope #1's countdown overlay unchanged. Against the previous renderer.js/agent-hud.html, 8 of the 9 fail; with the fix all pass, plus the bridge's 18.

  FOLLOW-UP, same day, direct request ('make agent window scrollable so i can read the overflow data'): it was meant to scroll already -- .hud said overflow-y: auto -- but the same rule said overflow: hidden five lines later, and the later declaration wins, so long content was clipped. Also, as a flex column it squashed its sections to fit rather than overflowing. DECISION: drop the stray overflow: hidden, give sections flex: none, and make the header sticky (it holds the status and is the drag handle, so it must not scroll away). Test: test_overflowing_content_scrolls_with_the_wheel_and_the_header_stays overfills the 600 px window, wheels it, and checks scrollTop moved and the header stayed at the top; it fails on the old CSS.
- [x] `scope2_launch_opens_the_agent_workspace` -- 2026-10-05, reported by the user testing Scope #2 from Electron: 'Launch Test Tools only launches Excel worksheet instead of actual workspace in the website', and after Play 'it launches a different online workspace'. Clarified: 'When I press Launch Mockups I want that specific part to where the Agent will work over once I click Play.'

  ROOT CAUSE: two buttons, two browsers. Play's automate.py --show always launched its OWN Playwright Chromium. An earlier fix had removed the portal from Launch because opening it there made a dead duplicate window -- which left Launch showing only the spreadsheet.

  DECISION: one window, shared, the same shape as Scope #1 (the user opens the target, the agent works in it). New components/scope2/executor/workspace.py: open_workspace() starts Chromium on the portal with a DevTools port (9333, own profile, detached); attach() connects to it over CDP and returns its portal tab. Launch (main.js TEST_MOCKUPS) now opens the sheet AND open_workspace.py. runner.run(show=True) attaches first and falls back to launching its own browser only when no workspace is open -- or when the CDP handshake fails, so a slow connect never crashes Play. Attached runs only disconnect at the end, so the filled window stays on screen without the 10-minute hold. Measurement runs (show=False) never attach, so the experiment stays headless and isolated.

  FOUND WHILE BUILDING: scanner.CHROMIUM pins ms-playwright/chromium-1208, which does not exist on this machine (1234/1243 installed). The runner silently coped by letting Playwright choose; Launch needs a real path, so workspace.chromium_path() asks Playwright instead of guessing a folder. Also measured: the CDP handshake sometimes exceeded 3 s here, so the attach timeout is 15 s with the fallback above.

  TESTS: tests/scope2/test_workspace.py (12) -- unit (tab picking, launch args, no second window when one is open, handshake failure falls back, no workspace -> None, static guard that Launch opens the workspace), integration (a real headless Chromium launched by open_workspace is found by attach and survives disconnect), E2E (runner under show fills 3 rows inside the already-open window, values read back from that window, window still alive, never waits on stdin). 8 consecutive green runs; Scope #2 suite 252 passed.

- [ ] `scope2_llm_tier_fills_too_few` -- 2026-10-05, reported with the item above: after Play it 'only fills a few spaces'. Measured, not guessed: dry-running automate.py headless on all eight portals and scoring against eval/ground_truth.py -- 27 right, 0 wrong, 8 missed. Course (from PROGRAM) is missed on EVERY portal, and on V2 Final Rating (from FINAL GRADE) is missed, which also skips the Academic Standing rule. Cause: the LLM tier (resolver/fallback.py) asks qwen2.5-7b per field with a prompt that pushes NONE ('if none clearly does, or two fit equally well, answer NONE'); with PROGRAM = 'BS Information Systems' and placeholder 'BS Computer Science' it still answers NONE. August runs (pre scope2_source_llm_like_scope1) filled Course.

  TRIED: (a) softer wording -> 30 right but 1 WRONG (Final Rating <- FINAL) and answers changed run to run at temperature 0; (b) lettered multiple choice -> stable across 3 runs, 33 right, 1 missed, but 2 WRONG (Final Rating <- FINAL; V3 Adviser <- No.). A wrong value is worse than an empty one, so neither shipped. OPEN: pick an approach that fills Course without ever writing a wrong value.

The 10.4 s was the MiniLM model LOAD (8.9 s timed alone), paid at every Play even with every embedding cached, because features/extractor.py calls encoders.available(), which loads the model to answer. Scope #1 never loads it at run time: its transformer's _embed_text() is a hash; sentence-transformers is only used by its training code.

Order is Scope #1's: the field's label is looked up among the sheet's column names (VIA_SOURCE = 'source', Scope #1's word, so both HUDs say the same thing); the LLM is asked per field only for what the source cannot name, and may answer NONE. The matcher code stays in the repo for eval/, which measures it.

  DECISION 2 -- make the lookup read labels like a person. Before, plain lookup decided 0 of V0's 5 fields, because every label carries a format hint ('Year 1-5', 'Grade 0-100'). Tokens containing a digit are dropped from names (label_words). And a column is only offered to a field its values fit (candidate_columns, now used by the source tier too), so V6b's 'Grade 1.00-5.00' never takes the 0-100 FINAL GRADE.

  DECISION 3 -- change the portal: Midterm and Final fields added to every variant but V2 (the relabelled stress case, kept hard like Scope #1's RELABELED intake) and V6b (a 1.00-5.00 scale, where 0-100 exams would contradict it). The sheet always had MIDTERM and FINAL columns with nowhere to go. V0 is now source 4 : LLM 1 (Course <- PROGRAM is the one the sheet names differently), plus Remarks by rule.

  SPEED FOUND ON THE WAY, measured: the LLM step's 7.7 s was 6 s of CONNECTING -- 'localhost' made Windows try IPv6 first (~2 s per fresh process; LM Studio listens on IPv4) and importing the openai package cost 2.2 s. The two questions took 0.2 s each. LMStudioAsker now talks to 127.0.0.1 with urllib: 7.7 s -> 0.6 s. The 5 s countdown was removed -- Scope #1 needs it to click its window; this script drives its own browser. Result: browser opens at ~5 s instead of 24.2 s.

  REAL MODEL FAILURES FOUND BY RUNNING, each fixed generically: (a) asked alone about 'Recommendations optional', gemma-3-4b answered PROGRAM every time -- as it did for Course -- so both were refused and Course went empty. Listing the other fields in the prompt did not fix it (3/4 still PROGRAM). A per-COLUMN tie-break, asked once with only the claimants offered, picked Course 4/4; anything but exactly one claimant still refuses all. (b) On V2/V6b it put FINAL GRADE in the notes box; a one-option confirming question agreed with anything, so it was rejected; instead a textarea never receives a column of bare numbers (widget semantics, like the range check).

  THREE PRE-EXISTING CRASHES FOUND BY FILLING ALL 8 VARIANTS, verified against HEAD: (1) V2/V6b 'derived fields have unmet dependencies' -- the Remarks rule named V0's labels. New rules/rebind.py anchors the rule's input on the sheet column the demonstration filled it from (FINAL GRADE -> whatever field this mapping fills from it) and its output on the field offering both outcomes; no unique answer skips the rule out loud. (2) V2 'no column header matches Student ID' -- row alignment named V0's header; PortalSheet.printed_column now finds the key/name column by what it prints. (3) V4 wrote every value one column right -- the no-accessible-name fallback used column_index + 1, but column_index already counts the checkbox column.

  RESULT, graded against the portal's truth keys (read only by the grader): 34/35 correct across the 8 variants, 1 wrong fill -- V2's 'Final Rating 0-100' <- FINAL, the same decoy the matcher also missed. Source decides 27 mappings, the LLM 8 (77% source). With the LLM off: 27 correct, 0 wrong fills. All 8 variants fill and verify 2/2 rows end to end, with and without the LLM.

  ALSO: rules/options.py (outcome -> option) no longer calls the encoder, the last place a run could load MiniLM. The HUD shows 'source'/'LLM' per mapping and lists each empty field once with the LLM's reason. The registry entry dropped the matcher checkpoint (Play would have passed the removed --matcher flag).

Scope #2: 247 passing. Because lookup runs first, it overrode the matcher. ---

### Scope #3 — Email / Ticket Triage
Decision-making / conditional behavior — the strongest personalization claim
(two users triage differently).

**Concrete shape decided (2026-08-15)**: an email arrives, and the agent
decides — based on how *this* user has actually handled similar messages
before, not a fixed script — what it needs: routed into
[Scope #1](#task-list)'s form-filler, routed into [Scope #2](#task-list)'s
sheet-matcher, replied to directly, forwarded, flagged for a person, or left
alone. Then it confirms the outcome by reply. The decision itself is the
actual claim; connecting Scope #1 and #2 is a side effect of it, not the
point.

**CHOSEN APPROACH, implemented (2026-08-16), from that exact spec:** Inbox
Router — reads Gmail via OAuth2, learns a per-sender pattern profile
passively from the Sent folder (no manual labeling), classifies each
incoming email with a rules-first/LLM-fallback hybrid, and routes it to
Scope #1's form-filler, Scope #2's sheet-matcher, a reply, a forward, a flag,
or leave-alone — draft-only, never auto-send, same standing rule as
`run_task.py`: real outward action is the user's confirmation, not
automatic. Polling, not Gmail push/watch (this app has no server
infrastructure to receive a webhook). See `components/inbox_router/`. **This
is NOT gated on Action-Space (Big Three #2) or Control-Flow (Big Three
#3)** — it's a legitimate alternative that unblocks Scope #3 rather than
waiting on them.

  IPC to hand off rather than duplicating it. Phase A (`MockGmailClient`,
  zero live credentials) implemented; Phase B (`RealGmailClient`) is a
  drop-in swap once a Google Cloud OAuth client exists — see
  `components/inbox_router/gmail_client.py`'s `get_gmail_client()`.

Direct instruction: follow Scope #1's own architecture, not the shared `components/shared/run_recorder.py` module built for the Scope #1/#2 unification effort (`execution_scope_unification`) — that module currently exists only on the unmerged `experiment/scope-unification` branch, and this work was explicitly done on `master` instead, independent of that branch. `router.py`'s `_record_session_metrics()` mirrors `run_task.py`'s own `finally:`-block pattern: a local metrics computation + a plain inline `try/except` JSONL append to `data/output/run_metrics.jsonl`, never raising on write failure.

Direct choice, discussed and confirmed: one row per *session*, written on shutdown, accumulating counters across the whole session's lifetime — not per-poll (too noisy) and not on a timer (adds complexity with no session on this project to justify it yet). Row shape: `messages_routed`, `decisions` (by type — `route_scope1`/`route_scope2`/`reply`/`forward`/`flag`/`leave_alone`), `layer` (rule vs LLM split, direct analog to Scope #1's Transformer/LLM dependency metrics), `confirmed`/`overridden` counts (overridden is a direct accuracy proxy — the model got it wrong, a human corrected it, same idea as Scope #1's manual-intervention rate), and `avg_confidence`. Root cause, confirmed by comparing thread IDs directly: the 5 sent messages referenced `thread-101` through `thread-105`, none of which existed anywhere in the fixture's `inbox` array (which only had `thread-001` through `thread-006`) — `observe_sent_history()`'s thread-correlation logic is correct, the fixture data was just internally disconnected.

The 5 new historical threads are pre-marked processed via the real `MockGmailClient.mark_processed()` call path (not a hand-written state file) so they don't wrongly resurface as new triage items in a live run. `tests/test_inbox_router.py` untouched and still 32/32 — its tests build their own isolated fixtures via `_write_fixture()`, never touching the committed file.
- [x] `scope3_record_train_output_pipeline` — **Added 2026-08-25, direct request** ("develop Scope #3 further in a new branch. Record -> Train -> Output. That output must have that reasoning, fast fill, and navigation"), on branch `feature/scope3-record-train-output`. Scope #3 previously had no trained model at all -- decisions came entirely from `RuleLayer` (deterministic) and `LLMClassifier` (LLM fallback), with `PatternProfile` learning passively from Sent-folder history. This gives it the same three-stage shape Scope #1 uses: **Record** (`decision_recorder.py` appends one labeled example per real Confirm/Override click, plus a one-time `bootstrap_from_sent.py` seed from existing Sent-folder correlation), **Train** (`train_inbox_agent.py`, same CLI shape as `scripts/train.py`, fits a small classifier on recorded examples), **Output** (`inbox_agent.py`'s `InboxAgent`, which replaces `InboxRouter`'s inline rule-then-LLM branching with one `decide()` call).

  Feature extraction (`inbox_features.py`) directly mirrors `components/scope2/features/extractor.py`'s role: a Transformer-based sentence embedding (reused from `components/scope2/features/encoders.py`, not a second copy of the model) produces per-decision centroid-similarity scores, combined with `PatternProfile` ratios, `RuleLayer` keyword-match signals, and structural features into one small fixed-length vector. The trained model (`inbox_model.py`'s `InboxDecisionNet`) directly mirrors `components/scope2/model/matcher.py`'s `Matcher` shape and reasoning -- small on purpose, since a large network would memorize the handful of examples expected early on rather than learn from them.

  `InboxAgent` fast-fills (acts alone, no LLM call) when the trained model's top prediction is >=75% confident (`RuleLayer`'s own existing default, not `agent.py`'s deliberately-disabled `1.01`); otherwise it reasons via the same `RuleLayer` -> `LLMClassifier` chain that already existed, now internal to `InboxAgent` rather than separate legacy plumbing sitting behind a new model. A `route_scope1`/`route_scope2` fast-fill additionally requires a keyword-verified capsule name -- a confident-but-unverifiable route prediction falls through to reasoning rather than guessing.

  Navigation deliberately deferred, direct instruction ("let's not mind it right now") -- stays a named property of the eventual output, concrete shape left to a follow-up.

  RuleLayer gained one public rename (`_match_capsule` -> `match_capsule`) since `inbox_features.py` and `inbox_agent.py` both now call it directly -- checked first that nothing outside `routing_rules.py` referenced the private name.

Full suite: 1346 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed.

The idea that stuck: Scope #3 isn't a third task competing with Scope #1/#2 for attention — it's the layer that watches a real signal (the inbox) continuously and autonomously *triggers* Scope #1 or #2 automation, using the already-trained `InboxDecisionNet` classifier from `scope3_record_train_output_pipeline`, instead of waiting for a human to click Play.   **Hard, non-negotiable safety boundary, matching this project's own "only the user runs live tasks" rule**: a `route_scope1` decision (real mouse/keyboard on the real screen) is only ever logged to `data/needs_attention.jsonl` — `handle_entry()` never calls `popen` for it, full stop. Only `route_scope2` (Playwright's own isolated Chromium) can be auto-dispatched. `watch()`'s `stop_when_idle` parameter replaced an earlier draft that faked "run once" behavior with `max_iterations=10_000_000` and a no-op sleep — caught and fixed before ever running it, since that would have spun a tight loop up to 10 million times instead of exiting promptly. 10 new tests in `test_autonomous_watcher.py`, including `test_route_scope1_surfaces_but_never_launches_anything` — asserts `popen.calls == []`, the load-bearing safety property.

  **Superseded 2026-08-29 by the `scope3_redefined_choices_and_schedule` work**: the `route_scope1`/`route_scope2` mechanism and `dispatch_scope2()` described above were removed entirely; Scope #3 no longer routes to Scope #1/#2 at all.
- [x] `scope3_learned_autonomous_reply` — **2026-08-27, direct request** ("Let's do the Email thing but I think it's best if we just don't just click buttons or what not but also reply to certain emails" → "Send these replies on its own" → "I don't want that hardcoded... I want it to be learned by the Agent"). Extends `scope3_autonomous_orchestration` with a third autonomous action: replying, not just dispatching. Agreed 3-step plan, step 1 shipped this pass:

  1. **Capture real reply text (done).** `confirm_suggestion()`/`override_decision()` in `router.py` no longer call `llm.draft_message()` to generate a fake AI reply and drop it straight into a Gmail draft — that directly violated this project's "never send AI-authored text, only the user's own words" rule. Both methods now build the draft from the human's own typed `reply_body` (empty string if none given — never invents placeholder text), and record it via the new `reply_recorder.py` (mirrors `decision_recorder.py`'s shape exactly). Fixed a real gap along the way: `override_decision()` previously created no draft at all when overriding to "reply"/"forward", silently dropping the human's typed text. `local_server.py` threads `reply_body` through `/api/confirm` and `/api/override` so the HTTP path (Inbox Dispatch's real webpage) can carry it too. 84 tests across `test_reply_recorder.py` (new, 6), `test_inbox_router.py`, `test_local_server.py`; full suite 1445 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed.
  2. **Train a real small model on captured replies (not started).** Same `InboxDecisionNet`-shaped approach as the existing decision classifier — trained weights pick the best-fitting past reply for a new email, not hand-written text-similarity matching.
  3. **Autonomous sending, gated on that model's confidence (not started).** Wires into `autonomous_watcher.py`'s `handle_entry()` for `reply` decisions — sends only when confident, never guesses.

  Frontend gap closed same day: `local_ui`'s detail view now has a real `<textarea>` in the reply bar, shown whenever the suggested decision or the selected override is "reply"/"forward" (the only two cases the backend does anything with `reply_body`); Confirm/Override send whatever's actually typed. The pencil "Reply" icon no longer fires an override immediately (it couldn't send anything meaningful without a text box) — it now switches the override dropdown to "Reply" and focuses the textarea. Smoke-tested against the real running server (fetched `/` and `/app.js` from `localhost:8765`, confirmed the new markup/wiring is actually served). Step 1 of the 3-step plan is now fully reachable by a human through the page, not just the backend.

  **Steps 2 and 3, added 2026-08-28, direct request ("We need to finish Scope #3 ASAP").** Step 2 is a real matching problem, not a classification one: given a new email, which of the (growing, unbounded) set of past real replies fits best — a different shape than `InboxDecisionNet`'s fixed 6-class output. New module `reply_features.py` mirrors `inbox_features.py`'s reuse of `components/scope2/features/encoders.py`, but produces a 3-dim per-*pair* feature vector (`context_cosine_sim`, `same_sender`, `subject_overlap`) instead of a per-message one. `reply_model.py`'s `ReplyMatchNet` (same save/load + version+dims mismatch guard shape as `inbox_model.py`) turns those raw signals into one learned match score — the similarity numbers are hand-computed inputs, the weights that combine them into a decision are trained, same rule this project holds everywhere else. `train_reply_model.py` (`MIN_EXAMPLES = 6`, same bar as the decision model) builds training pairs the standard way a matching model gets trained without explicit "bad fit" labels: each real reply is a positive pair with the email it actually answered (the strongest signal available — real behavior, not a guess), and negatives come from pairing that same email against a handful of other examples' context, sampled at random (standard in-batch negative sampling). `reply_agent.py`'s `ReplyAgent.suggest_reply()` is the Output step: cold-start-safe exactly like `InboxAgent` (missing/corrupt checkpoint or zero recorded examples → empty suggestion, never invented text), scores every stored example against a new email, and only returns a candidate's real `reply_body` verbatim if it clears the confidence bar — direct request confirmed this bar should match the existing 0.75 fast-fill threshold, not a separate one.

  Step 3 (autonomous drafting) surfaced a real, pre-existing, deliberate boundary before any code was touched: `gmail_client.py`'s own docstring states outright that `GmailClientBase` "deliberately has no send()/send_message() method anywhere" — no code path in this project can put a real email in front of a person without a human's hand on it. Flagged directly rather than silently building past it; direct decision confirmed: keep that wall. So step 3 is auto-*drafting*, not auto-*sending* — `autonomous_watcher.py`'s `handle_entry()` gained a `reply`/`forward` branch (both `reply_agent`/`gmail_client` params must be supplied together to activate it, otherwise it's a no-op, so nothing changes for callers that don't opt in) that asks `ReplyAgent.suggest_reply()` and, only on a confident answer, calls the *already-existing* `create_draft()` — no new capability was added to `gmail_client.py` at all, this reuses exactly the same method `router.py`'s own `confirm_suggestion()`/`override_decision()` call for a human-typed reply, with the identical to/subject construction (`Fwd:`/`Re:`, `forward_to` vs. sender). Every auto-draft is logged to `data/autonomous_drafts.jsonl` (message, confidence, which past example it reused) so it's always auditable without asking. A human still has to open Gmail and hit send themselves — the boundary genuinely never moved.

  Known follow-up, not yet done: the auto-drafted entry's `draft_id`/suggested text aren't written back into `InboxRouter`'s own pending-entry history, so Inbox Dispatch's page doesn't yet show "a draft is already waiting" — a human who later opens that email and confirms it themselves could end up with two drafts for the same thread. Real Gmail draft creation itself is fully working and tested; this is a UI-visibility gap, not a correctness gap in the drafting logic.

  TDD throughout: `test_reply_features.py` (9), `test_reply_model.py` (7), `test_train_reply_model.py` (4), `test_reply_agent.py` (6), plus 8 new tests in `test_autonomous_watcher.py` (17 total in that file) covering confident auto-draft, forward's `Fwd:`/`forward_to` handling, low-confidence and missing-message fallthrough, the "both params required to activate" contract, and that this doesn't touch the separate Scope #1 safety boundary.

  **Recording routed through the real Electron DemoRecorder, 2026-08-28, direct instruction** ("Also any instance of recording has to go through the Electron application's recording part."). The 3-step plan above captured reply text through a plain web form's `fetch()` calls — real, working, but not a *recording*, and inconsistent with this project's rule that every demonstration feeding training goes through the same Record step Scope #1/#2 use. Six-task plan, all shipped, every task's review came back clean (most after exactly one fix round for a real bug found in review):

  1. **`components/recorder/recorder.py` gains a real `trace_type="web"` path.** A correction made while writing the plan: the class Electron's Recording button actually drives is `DemoRecorder` — not `ScreenObserver`, a different, unused class living in the same file. `DemoRecorder` now imports `WebObserver` (already existed, previously unwired) the same lazy way it already imports `ExcelObserver`/`UIAutomationObserver`. When `trace_type=="web"` it launches its OWN real, visible Chromium via `WebObserver(headless=False).connect(url=url)` — not attaching to an externally-opened browser over CDP, which an earlier design draft tried and was wrong, since `shell.openExternal` gives no launch-flag control. Bug found and fixed in review: if `connect()` failed *after* the browser was already launched (e.g. `page.goto()` throwing on a bad URL), the code raised `RuntimeError` without ever calling `disconnect()`, orphaning a real Chromium process — fixed by disconnecting before raising. 7 new tests, full suite 1493 passed.

  2. **`local_ui/app.js`'s `openMessage()` tags the reply box with the open email.** One line, `replyBody.name = email.message_id;`, so a recorded session can later identify which email a typed reply belongs to. Tested with a real Playwright browser test. 1 new test, full suite 1494 passed.

  3. **`reply_trace_translator.py` (new) turns a finished recording session into real (email, reply) pairs**, recorded through the existing, unmodified `reply_recorder.record_reply_example()`. Bug found and fixed in review, a real algorithm error: the original design compared each step's state against the *next file's* state, with a special-cased "or it's the last step" branch — this directly contradicted the plan's own test case and could silently drop legitimately-submitted replies. Root cause: `DemoRecorder` already captures a before/after pair (`state`/`next_state`) *within a single step file* for every triggered action — the submitting click's own `next_state` already shows the textarea gone, so there's no need to look at another file or special-case "last step" at all. A genuine design correction, not a workaround. 6 new tests, full suite reached 1500 passed after the fix. (The "textarea gone in `next_state`" rule was itself replaced in the final fix wave below — it was still the wrong signal, just for a different reason.)

  4. **`app/recorder_bridge.py`'s `Bridge.start()` gains `trace_type`/`url` params**, defaulting to the old hardcoded `"form_filling"`/`""` so every existing caller is unaffected, threaded through to `DemoRecorder(...)` and the `cmd=="start"` stdin dispatch. Calls `ensure_server_running()` (imported from `automate_inbox.py`, not duplicated) before constructing a web-mode recorder. Bug found and fixed in review: `ensure_server_running()` raises `SystemExit` (a `BaseException`, not `Exception`) on timeout — an unguarded call would have crashed the entire `recorder_bridge.py` subprocess, which also owns unrelated recording state, instead of failing gracefully. Fixed with `try/except (Exception, SystemExit)` + `emit("error", ...)`, matching the file's own existing error-handling convention elsewhere. Full suite reached 1505 passed after the fix.

  5. **`app_electron/preload.js` + `main.js`: the `recorder-start` IPC handler infers `trace_type="web"`/`url=capsule.url` automatically** when the currently-loaded capsule is Inbox Dispatch — no new UI button. Bug found and fixed in review, more significant than the others: the plan (written from an older design doc) assumed Inbox Dispatch's registry entry has `kind: "url"` — but the REAL, current `tasks/registry.json` has it as `kind: "script"` (changed earlier in the project's history when it started running a real automation script, `automate_inbox.py`, instead of just opening a page externally). The original check `capsule.kind === "url"` would therefore NEVER have fired for the one capsule this whole feature exists to serve, silently defeating the feature entirely. Fixed by checking `capsule.url` presence instead of `capsule.kind` — `WorkflowCapsule.url` is an independent dataclass field, and it's the ONLY capsule in the registry that has a `url` field at all, so the check is unambiguous and also more general: any future web-driving capsule gets correct behavior automatically.

  **Known follow-up, not fixed in this plan (real, named gap):** the same now-stale `capsule.kind === "url"` assumption that caused the Task 5 bug may also appear elsewhere — flagged at `app_electron/main.js` around lines 679 and 902, and `app_electron/renderer/renderer.js` around lines 599, 610, and 935. Outside this plan's scope to fix, not yet verified, worth checking in a future pass.

  Two harmless minor gaps, also deferred (not blocking, not fixed): `local_ui/app.js`'s `closeMessage()` doesn't reset `replyBody.name` back to empty (harmless today — `openMessage()` always overwrites it on next open and the field is hidden while no message is open); a couple of the reply-trace-translator's tests ended up exercising the same code path after the algorithm fix (redundant coverage, not a defect).

  All 6 tasks complete, every task's individual review clean. Last commit of the six-task pass: `4307689c`.

  **Final whole-branch review + fix wave, 2026-08-28.** Reviewing the six tasks *together* — not one at a time — found two Critical bugs that lived entirely in the seams between tasks. Both were invisible per-task because every task's tests used fakes or hand-built dicts, so no test ever ran two real objects against each other. Named honestly because the lesson generalizes: **per-task green tests say nothing about the joints between tasks.**

  - **C1 — the feature recorded nothing, ever.** Task 2 set `replyBody.name = message_id`; Task 3 read it back off the element's `label`/`text`. But `WebObserver._extract_elements()` builds that label from a priority chain (`aria-label` > `placeholder` > `title` > `name` > `inner_text`), and the reply textarea has a real, deliberate placeholder — so `name` was *unreachable*. The translator got the placeholder sentence, `get_message()` returned `None`, and every step was silently skipped. Fixed additively: the element dict gains its own dedicated `name` key, so the human-readable label and the machine identity stop competing for one slot. Reordering the chain would have broken every other element's label; deleting the placeholder would have broken real UX text.
  - **C2 — every recorded state was empty, silently.** Playwright's sync API is thread-bound (greenlet). `DemoRecorder.__init__` built and connected `WebObserver` on the *caller's* thread, but every snapshot runs on `self._worker_thread` — so each one raised `greenlet.error`, swallowed twice (`WebObserver.snapshot()` and `_request_snapshot()` both catch bare `Exception`). Same class of bug as the COM/UIA one already documented in `_worker()`, and given the identical fix: the thread-sensitive setup moved to the top of `_worker()` itself. `__init__` now blocks on an event so its "raise if the browser won't connect" contract still holds, the web-case final drain + `disconnect()` moved into `_worker()`'s post-loop (the only thread allowed to touch Playwright), and `run()` joins the worker before returning.
  - **I3/I4 — the translator trusted a negative signal.** "Textarea absent from `next_state`" was treated as proof of submission. Back and Archive hide it too, so abandoned drafts were recorded as sent; and a missing/empty `next_state` (exactly what C2 produced) trivially satisfied it, i.e. it failed *open*. Replaced with a positive requirement: `next_state` must actually contain the UI's real success status message. `local_ui/index.html`'s snackbar gained `role="status"`, and `WebObserver`'s selector now collects `[role='status']` — a general, standards-defined addition alongside the ARIA roles it already reads, not a page-specific hack.
  - **I5 — a leaked server process.** `Bridge.start()` called `ensure_server_running()` and threw away its return value; a non-`None` return means *this* call spawned `local_server.py`, and nothing ever terminated it, so it outlived Electron. Handle now stored and terminated on stop/shutdown/failed-start. `None` (someone else's server) is still never touched.

  New tests written specifically to be the ones that would have caught these: a real-Playwright-browser `WebObserver` test asserting `name == "m1"` survives a non-empty `placeholder` (`tests/test_web_observer_element_fields.py`), and a thread-identity test asserting `connect`/`snapshot`/`disconnect` all land on the worker thread and never on the caller's (`tests/test_demo_recorder_web_observer.py`). Both were verified RED against the pre-fix code before being made green. Also corrected here: the per-task suite counts above, which had task 2's number wrong (1494, not 1500 — it added exactly 1 test), and the "final commit" SHA.

  End state: 1520 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed.

  **Regressed and re-fixed 2026-09-01 — Stop Recording was a dead end.** Direct report, testing the Electron Recorder live: "That's not the pipeline oh my God. We need to record via the Electron application," followed by "we need to utilize the Recorder because that's what we actually use and one of our hooks." Root-caused: a web recording (Inbox Dispatch) correctly captured a real session on Stop, but `reply_trace_translator.py` — the module built in this exact node to convert a session into real training data — was never actually called by the app. It only ever ran by hand from a terminal, pointed at the exact session folder, which nobody was doing; the standing rule that recording must go through Electron had silently regressed to "captures a recording, does nothing with it."

  Fixed in `app/recorder_bridge.py`: `Bridge._run()`'s `finally` block (the same place `session_dir`/`steps` were already being read for the `"saved"` event) now calls `translate_session(session_dir, get_gmail_client())` itself the moment a web recording stops, guarded to `trace_type == "web"` and `steps > 0` so form_filling recordings and empty sessions are untouched. A translation failure is caught and logged, never crashes the bridge or blocks the save. The `"saved"` event now carries `trace_type`/`examples_written`, and `renderer.js` reports the real example count for a web recording instead of the "click Replay ×N" line, which never applied to a page recording anyway. 4 new tests in `test_recorder_bridge_web_recording.py`. Full suite: 1591 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed. Commit `d8794e23` on this branch.

  Noted, not yet resolved: `decision_recorder.record_example()`/`reply_recorder.record_reply_example()` also fire directly and unconditionally inside `router.py`'s `confirm_suggestion()`/`override_decision()` on every real click to the live Inbox Dispatch page — regardless of whether an Electron recording session is active at all. This silent path still technically bypasses "recording has to go through Electron," and is a separate, real question (keep as an always-on backup capture, or gate it behind an active session?) not yet decided or touched.

  **Second regression found immediately after, same session, live testing.** Direct report: "The only recording pathway is still form_filling." Two real, separate bugs found, not one: (1) `populateOutDirOptions()`'s filter excluded every script-kind capsule outright — including Inbox Dispatch, which genuinely does record via the Recorder (that's this whole feature), unlike `Sheet-to-Portal Matcher` (also script-kind, but trains its own matcher in-process and never records here at all). (2) The more load-bearing bug: selecting an option in "Save to" only ever set the dropdown's own value (an output-folder string) — it never touched `currentCapsuleName`, the actual thing `main.js`'s `recorder-start` handler reads to decide `trace_type="web"` vs `"form_filling"`. That variable only ever got set by loading a capsule into the Play panel, on a completely different tab, with nothing in the Recorder panel showing or controlling it — so even after fixing (1), picking "Inbox Dispatch" from Save-to would still silently record `form_filling`.

  Fixed both in `app_electron/renderer/renderer.js`: the filter now includes any capsule that isn't `kind="url"` and is either non-script or carries a `url` (so `Sheet-to-Portal Matcher` stays correctly excluded, `Inbox Dispatch` is now included and labeled "Inbox Dispatch (real page)" in the list). Selecting an option now calls `loadCapsuleIntoSlot()` — the same function a Tasks-page click already uses — so choosing a capsule from Save-to is what actually makes it the recording target, closing the gap between "what the dropdown shows" and "what actually gets recorded." Verified the filter directly against the real `registry.json` before committing. Commit `e6d60bd8` on this branch.

Replaced three buttons/IPC handlers/preload calls with one: `btnLaunchTestTools` / `launch-test-tools` / `capsulesAPI.launchTestTools()`. For `form_filling`/`Sheet-to-Portal Matcher`, behaves exactly as Launch mockups always did; for Inbox Dispatch, one click now opens Practice Inbox, `schedule.txt` in Notepad, and the Cold Email page together. Fixed a real inconsistency along the way: View Schedule used to show unconditionally for every task even though `schedule.txt` is Scope #3-specific content no other task ever writes to — it's now only opened as part of Inbox Dispatch's own bundle, matching the gating Cold Email already correctly used. Commit `b06da057` on this branch.

  **Merged 2026-09-01, direct request: "Can we put Cold Email inside the application too? I'm trying to unify the Email webpage."** Cold Email used to be its own standalone page (`/cold-email/`) — now a real Gmail-style sidebar section inside the main Inbox Dispatch page itself, matching how real Gmail switches Inbox/Starred/etc within one page instead of navigating elsewhere. `local_ui/index.html` gained a "Cold Email" nav item and its own list/detail markup, reusing existing shared classes (`.row-item`, `.detail-view`, `.reply-textarea`, `.btn-primary`) plus two small new pieces (`.compose-form`, `.detail-target-email`) ported from the removed page's stylesheet. `local_ui/app.js`'s `setView()` now handles a third `"cold_email"` state, always closing any open detail view first when switching sections, same as real Gmail. Removed the standalone `cold_email/` page entirely; `local_server.py` keeps its `/cold-email/api/*` routes unchanged, now consumed by the merged view. Updated Electron's "Launch Test Tools" to stop opening the now-gone separate URL. Rewrote the standalone page's real-browser tests to navigate the merged page instead, plus one new test proving switching sections never leaves the wrong view showing. Full suite: 1589 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed. Commit `774be6ac` on this branch.

  **Two real bugs found and fixed 2026-09-01 by actually running Scope #3 live**, not just via tests — direct request after "we don't have time to record" got clarified into "show Scope #3 running for real like the way it does on Scope #1." Ran `automate_inbox.py --commit` against the real server and watched it "confirm" the exact same email five times in a row instead of advancing through the inbox — a real defect, not a demo artifact, caught specifically because it was run live rather than assumed working from passing tests.

  Root-caused with direct API calls, not guessed: `router.py`'s `_update_history_entry()`/`_find_history_entry()` both matched the FIRST history row for a given `message_id`, but `pending_entries()` reads "latest wins" (the last matching row). A message polled across multiple separate server restarts can end up with more than one "pending" history row for the same id (found live: `mock-001` had accumulated 6 rows across sessions, one genuinely `confirmed` and five stale `pending` duplicates) — confirming it then silently patched an old, already-resolved row while the current, actually-reported-as-pending row never changed, so the message could never really be confirmed. Fixed both methods to target the latest matching row, consistent with `pending_entries()`'s own dedup rule. New regression test reproduces the exact corrupted-history shape directly.

  Second, related root cause found in the same pass: `app_electron/main.js`'s `ensureLocalServerRunning()` only ever checked its own in-memory handle before spawning `local_server.py` — never whether something was actually already listening on the port. A server started manually, or left over from an earlier Electron session, binds the same port fine (`SO_REUSEADDR`), so every one of those went undetected — the actual root cause behind every "two `local_server.py` processes silently splitting traffic" incident this whole session, not something that could be fully fixed by just killing stray processes each time. Added a real HTTP liveness check before spawning, mirroring `automate_inbox.py`'s own `ensure_server_running()` — the identical fix, already proven correct on the Python side, now applied consistently on the JS side too.

  Verified live, not just via the suite: after the fix, a clean run of `automate_inbox.py --commit --limit 5` processed 5 distinct emails (not the same one five times) and the real pending count dropped from 27 to 22. Full suite: 1590 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed. Commit `c863010d` on this branch.

  **`automate_cold_email.py` added 2026-09-01, direct clarification: "The boss task list is our Scope #3's version of Scope #1's data entry list. We need Scope #3 boss task list thing."** Scope #1 has a real list it works through (`data_entry_intake.txt`); Scope #3's own equivalent is the boss' task list (`data/task_list.txt`) driving Cold Email — a completely separate thing from the regular inbox `automate_inbox.py` already plays. New script mirrors `automate_inbox.py`'s exact shape: opens the real merged page, switches to the Cold Email sidebar section, reads each real target off the real DOM (name, email, pre-filled subject). Never sends anything on its own — same "never invent text" rule, so every target is shown then left exactly as it was, the same way `automate_inbox.py` already leaves Reply/Forward/Schedule pending rather than blank-confirming them. Walks by a plain index rather than `automate_inbox.py`'s confirm-then-advance shape, since nothing here ever gets removed from the list automatically. Parametrized `automate_inbox.py`'s `print_countdown()` to accept a custom message instead of duplicating the whole function; `banner()`/`ensure_server_running()` reused as-is. 3 new real-browser tests, same real-HTTPServer-plus-real-Playwright-browser pattern already used elsewhere. Also added `components/inbox_router/data/runs/` to `.gitignore` — 14 generated run-log files had been accumulating untracked. Full suite: 1593 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed. Verified live: walked all 3 real targets from the actual `task_list.txt` fixture, created zero drafts. Commit `012868d8` on this branch.

Reply/Forward/Schedule are untouched and still require a human's own real typed words; this is the sole place in the entire project an LLM's own generated text is what actually gets sent.

  New `components/inbox_router/cold_email_llm.py`: `generate_cold_email(name, context_line)` reuses the same OpenAI-compatible LM Studio client construction `llm_classifier.py` already uses (`base_url="http://localhost:1234/v1"`, `api_key="lm-studio"`) — no new client library. Reads the loaded model's real id from LM Studio itself (`client.models.list()`) instead of hardcoding one, so it keeps working regardless of which model is actually loaded. Fails closed (returns `("", "")`) on any error — LM Studio unreachable, no model loaded, an unparseable response — same as every other LLM call in this project when nothing's available to answer.

A successful send removes the target from the list, so the loop re-reads the same row index on the next call rather than advancing past it, mirroring `automate_inbox.py`'s own confirm-then-re-read shape.

Full suite: 1603 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed. Commit `ab83d105` on this branch.

  **Sidebar fidelity fix, 2026-09-01, direct correction: "You know Gmail doesn't have a tab named Cold Email right?"** Real. Real Gmail's sidebar has no built-in category for it — only Inbox/Starred/Snoozed/Sent/Drafts/More are real system views; anything else is a user-created **label**, and Gmail visibly styles labels differently (a "Labels" divider, then a small colored square swatch, never mixed in among the system views with a pill-shaped icon). The Cold Email nav item had been placed and styled as if it were a fourth system view, which isn't what real Gmail does.

  Moved it below the existing system items (Inbox/Starred/Snoozed/Sent/Drafts/More) behind a new `.nav-section-label` "Labels" divider, and swapped its pill icon for a `.nav-label-swatch` — a plain 12×12 colored square, matching how a real custom label actually renders. No behavior changed — same `id="navColdEmail"`, same click handler, same `setView("cold_email")` — purely a visual-fidelity fix to how the label is presented, not what it does. Verified: all 8 existing Cold Email tests (routes + real-browser merge tests) still pass unchanged after the move; full suite confirmed clean.

  **Crash-recovery fix, 2026-09-01, live-found running the boss' task list for real.** After 3 real Cold Email sends completed successfully, the 4th call (checking for more targets) hit `TargetClosedError` — the browser/page had closed out from under the loop for reasons entirely outside either script's own control (nothing in the JS or Python ever closes it itself; this immediately followed a 7-minute full-suite run that itself launches many real Playwright browsers, plausibly resource contention). Both `automate_cold_email.py` and `automate_inbox.py` let that propagate as a raw, uncaught traceback, discarding the summary of real work already done — 3 real sends had already succeeded and were about to be reported, but the crash lost that report entirely.

  Both `main()` loops now catch `playwright.sync_api.Error` around their `while` loop, print a clear one-line message instead of a traceback, and still write whatever results were already collected to the run log. The final `browser.close()` is also guarded the same way, since closing an already-dead browser can raise the identical error a second time. 3 new tests (2 for Cold Email, 1 for the inbox script) using fake Playwright objects prove results collected before a mid-run crash survive and get written to the log, and a second closure error from `browser.close()` itself is also swallowed rather than escaping. Full suite: 1606 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed. Commit `d327b207` on this branch.

  **Sidebar removal, 2026-09-01, direct instruction after the earlier Gmail-label restyle still wasn't right.** "The Cold Email label tag is still there, please fix." → "Remove it from the sidebar entirely... the Agent will find it itself." A human never needs to see or click into Cold Email manually — it's driven by `automate_cold_email.py` walking the boss' task list, not by someone browsing a sidebar tab.

  `#navColdEmail` now carries a plain `hidden` attribute and no longer sits under a "Labels" divider with a colored swatch — that styling and its CSS (`.nav-section-label`/`.nav-label-swatch`) were removed entirely, since there's nothing left for it to visually distinguish. The button itself, and the Cold Email list/detail section it reveals, still exist in the DOM exactly as before — `automate_cold_email.py` still needs a real element to click to reach that section. Since the element is now hidden, its click needed `wait_for_selector(state="attached")` (the default "visible" would hang forever) and `click(force=True)` (bypasses Playwright's visibility check) — and every test driving this same page through the Cold Email section needed the identical fix (3 real-browser tests in `test_local_server.py`, `test_automate_cold_email.py`'s own fixture). No human should ever see or click anything to reach Cold Email — it's driven entirely by `automate_cold_email.py` walking the boss' task list.

Every test that used to click `#navColdEmail` (3 in `test_local_server.py`, 1 fixture in `test_automate_cold_email.py`) now calls the same `page.evaluate("setView('cold_email')")` instead.
