  **Direct pushback, same conversation: "I meant its still not finding the right view."** Re-read the already-collected log with fresh eyes rather than asking for a new run — found the real remaining symptom: the transformer's own pointer aimed at the exact same already-filled field's screen position (`'Years Continuously Insured'`, then later `'Suffix'`/`'Years at Address'`) on **3 consecutive steps in a row**, over and over, roughly 19 separate times across one ~148-step run. Each one was already being caught safely by the existing reclick guard (no wasted click, no data corruption — just a blind Tab), but the guard tracked nothing about *how many times in a row* this had just happened, and a blind Tab only gambles on OS focus-traversal order landing somewhere useful. When the same stuck position kept recurring, that gamble kept failing, producing exactly the "scattered, not sweeping cleanly" feeling the user described — a genuinely different problem from the scroll/reveal issue, now confirmed resolved separately.

  **Fixed with the same deterministic-redirect mechanism already proven for the low-confidence-fallback streak, applied to this streak too.** Added `_reclick_streak`/`_RECLICK_REDIRECT_LIMIT=2` (mirrors `_lowconf_fallback_streak`'s existing convention exactly — same reset points, same escalation shape): a single drift-back still gets a plain Tab (normal model uncertainty, usually self-corrects); a **second consecutive** drift onto the same already-handled field redirects straight to `find_visible_empty_target()`'s known, currently-visible, currently-empty target instead of another blind Tab-and-hope. Falls back to a plain Tab if no such target exists yet (e.g. mid-transition) rather than clicking nothing. Regression tests: `tests/test_reclick_streak_redirect.py` (3 new tests). Full suite 350 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Direct pushback, same conversation, on the underlying design rather than another symptom: "No, nothing's visible -> scroll down once. Wrong... Literally find the optimal view."** Right call — `decide()`'s actual rule until this point was "is at least one empty target visible right now" (WAIT the instant that's true, SCROLL only when it's false), which stops at the FIRST minimally-acceptable view instead of the BEST one. A tab with one field visible at the top and three more just below the fold would sit on the one-field view forever, never scrolling further to reach the four-field view, because "at least one visible" was already satisfied.

  **Redesigned `decide()` around a real "optimal view" definition, computed rather than approximated.** Added `optimal_view_counts(state, viewport_bottom, ...)` to `navigation_protocol.py`: gathers every remaining empty, not-yet-attempted, fillable target's y-position (including ones currently off-screen below the fold — `ui_observer.py` reports real bboxes for those regardless of current visibility; targets with `bbox top < 0`, i.e. already scrolled past, are excluded since this module only scrolls down and can't recover them), then runs a two-pointer max-points-in-a-fixed-width-window scan over the sorted positions to compute `best` — the most targets any single scroll position could ever show at once. `cur` is how many are visible right now (same filter, plus the real `IsOffscreen`-backed `visible` flag, which only means something for the CURRENT scroll offset). `decide()`'s new rule: `best == 0` -> `ADVANCE_TAB` immediately (nothing reachable anywhere, no reason to waste a scroll finding that out); `cur >= best` -> `WAIT` (this already IS the best view achievable, even if `best` is small); otherwise `SCROLL` toward it, with `dead_scroll_count` as the existing safety net if repeated scrolling can't actually close the gap (e.g. the pane can't move that far, or `best` was thrown off by a covered/stale element).

  **Same conversation, the user asked whether a single, precise scroll motion was possible — "You could do that before in the old Intern"** (a separate, since-deleted project, not recoverable). This repo's own git history had something structurally similar: `_scrollbar_drag` (commit `0ec5688a`, later removed as dead code when its then-caller was refactored away) — it walks UP from a real on-screen field through the UIA parent chain to find the actual scrollable pane, then fires one `ScrollPattern.Scroll(NoAmount, LargeIncrement)` call, a native page-scroll rather than a simulated mouse-wheel nudge. Revived as `_scroll_form_down_uia()`, generalized to drop the original's hardcoded field-name anchor list (`"Last Name"`, `"Email Address"`, ...) in favor of anchoring on whatever `editcontrol` labels are actually present in the current state — works on any form, not just this one. `_scroll_form_down` now tries this UIA route first, falling back to the existing count-based mouse-wheel approach (renamed `_scroll_form_down_wheel`, logic unchanged) if no scrollable pane is found or UIA raises. Deliberately does **not** verify the pane moved inside this function — that would reintroduce the "two independent observation cycles" bug class found and fixed three times already this project (verify-at-fill, the dead-scroll counter, the self-calibrating scroll's cursor-chaos regression); the caller's existing single observe-and-diff cycle in the SCROLL branch still owns that check. Regression tests: `tests/test_navigation_protocol.py::TestOptimalViewCounts` (5 new tests) and `::TestDecidePrefersMaximizingSimultaneousTargets` (2 new tests, including the exact scenario above — one visible field must NOT stop scrolling toward a three-field view just below it); `TestDecide`'s two dead-elements tests rewritten since an all-empty state now correctly means "nothing reachable" (`ADVANCE_TAB`) rather than "try scrolling anyway." Full suite 358 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "you didn't fill up shit."** Task completion 2.3% (4/176 fields). Log evidence, no guessing: at the very first step, before the transformer even acted, the new `decide()` correctly triggered a scroll (more targets existed than were visible) — but `_scroll_form_down_uia`'s `ScrollAmount.LargeIncrement` call overshot so far that **"First Name" — the field that should still have been reachable — vanished from the post-scroll snapshot entirely** (`[SCROLL-DIAG] tracked target 'First Name' vanished from the post-scroll snapshot`). Since this module only scrolls DOWN, never back up, that overshoot permanently lost access to whatever got skipped. Confirmed downstream: Navigation Protocol later tried clicking the same overshot area ("Home Phone" @ 1444,30) and got `validation=no_change` — nothing usable there anymore.

  **Root cause: `LargeIncrement` is a full native "page jump," much bigger than the old wheel-fallback's small -5/-15/-25 unit nudges** — reasonable in isolation, but combined with the new `decide()` (which already loops, re-scrolling as many times as needed until `cur == best`), one giant jump per call is the wrong shape: several small scrolls homing in on the optimal view is what the redesign actually wants, not one large blind one. **Fixed by switching to `ScrollAmount.SmallIncrement`** (and `SmallDecrement` for the unused up-direction, for consistency). Regression test added — this route had none before, being new, untestable-without-a-real-window code: `tests/test_scroll_form_down_uia.py` (3 new tests, `uiautomation`/`win32gui` faked via `sys.modules` injection — proves the actual `ScrollPattern.Scroll()` call receives `SmallIncrement` not `LargeIncrement`, that no scrollable pane found falls back cleanly, and that a missing UIA import falls back cleanly). Full suite 361 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Also flagged, not yet root-caused**: the same run's log showed the 'State' combobox filled correctly ('California') at step 11, then read back as blank at step 19 with no scroll or click on it in between — a real symptom, but with no evidence yet pointing at a specific cause. Tracked separately rather than guessed at.

  **Re-verified live immediately after the SmallIncrement fix, direct user report: "you're just scrolling slowly, filling nothing."** Log evidence: EVERY step in the run scrolled — none of them ever reached the transformer's own turn. `[SCROLL-DIAG]` showed the same field ('First Name') getting re-clicked 4 times in a row, ~25px apart each time, never typed into.

  **Root cause: two pieces of logic built for different rules were fighting each other.** The SCROLL branch still had OLD code (from before the optimal-view redesign) that clicked whatever field a scroll revealed and `continue`d straight back to the top of the loop, skipping the transformer's turn entirely. Under the OLD `decide()` ("is at least one target visible? then stop scrolling"), that was correct — the NEXT `decide()` call would see that field and return WAIT. Under the NEW `decide()` (WAIT only once `cur == best`, the genuinely maximal view), one revealed field usually isn't enough — so `decide()` kept returning SCROLL immediately again, and the stale click-and-continue kept firing on the same barely-moved field, forever, never once reaching the transformer.

  **Fixed by deleting the speculative click entirely.** The SCROLL branch now only scrolls, re-observes, and updates the dead-scroll counter based on `optimal_view_counts()`'s `cur` (the SAME metric `decide()` itself uses) — progress means `cur` increased, not "something is visible." No action is taken during scrolling; `decide()`, called fresh at the top of the loop on the post-scroll state, is now the single source of truth for when scrolling stops. Once it naturally returns WAIT, the existing per-step transformer/OPT2 flow (already correct, already verified) takes over clicking and filling.

  **`tests/test_dead_scroll_counter_requires_actionable_target.py` fully rewritten** — its old mirror functions tested the exact removed behavior (has_visible_empty_target-based reset, click-and-continue). New version mirrors the cur/best-based reset condition (6 tests, including the isolated regression scenario: one visible target staying at exactly one target must NOT reset the counter, only a genuine cur INCREASE counts as progress) plus a class documenting the click's removal. Full suite 362 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "It's just scrolling down and not filling anything."** Log evidence, and it's decisive: at step 1, `cur=16, best=22` — 16 of 22 targets on the tab were ALREADY visible, before any scrolling at all. But `decide()` (as redesigned two rounds ago) required `cur == best` before returning WAIT, so it kept scrolling to chase the last 6 fields one at a time — 8 consecutive scrolls (16→17→18→...→21). Then it hit a wall: 22-simultaneously-visible turned out to be physically impossible (scrolling far enough to bring the last field in pushed an earlier one off the top — `cur` capped at 21, never reached 22), `dead_scroll_count` maxed out, and the ENTIRE tab got abandoned via `ADVANCE_TAB` having filled **zero fields** — despite 16-21 targets sitting on screen the whole run.

  **Direct user correction, exactly naming the design flaw**: "There is nothing to improve yet because we haven't even filled up anything yet at all, why are we optimizing when the initial view is already optimized?" Right call. Requiring the GLOBAL maximum before acting is both pointless (a good-enough view is already actionable — there's no reason to hold out) and fragile (the maximum isn't guaranteed reachable at all, as the 21-vs-22 wall just proved).

  **Fixed by changing `decide()`'s WAIT condition from `cur >= best` back to `cur > 0`.** The moment ANYTHING actionable is visible, WAIT — full stop, act on it now. `optimal_view_counts()`'s `best` is still computed and still used, just narrowed to its actually-useful role: deciding whether scrolling is worth attempting at all when the CURRENT view has nothing (`cur == 0`, `best > 0` → SCROLL; `best == 0` → ADVANCE_TAB immediately), and sizing the give-up log message — never as a bar for stopping once something's already there. This is functionally close to the very first, pre-redesign rule (`has_visible_empty_target`), but now computed via the position-aware `optimal_view_counts` (which correctly excludes already-scrolled-past elements and respects the real `IsOffscreen`-backed `visible` flag) rather than the older first-match function.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_navigation_protocol.py`'s `TestDecidePrefersMaximizingSimultaneousTargets` class (which tested exactly the now-rejected "keep scrolling toward a denser view" behavior) replaced with `TestDecideActsOnWhateverIsAlreadyVisible` (3 tests, including a scaled-up reproduction of the actual live regression: 16 of 22 targets visible must still WAIT, not chase the remaining 6). `TestDecide`'s existing 5 tests needed ZERO changes — their cur/best boundary cases already matched the corrected behavior exactly, confirming this was a narrow, well-contained fix. Full suite 363 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "It's not working for fucks sake. It's using Tab to fucking navigate."** This run was genuinely much better — 20 real fields filled (11.4% completion, up from 2.3%), the scroll and decide() fixes both confirmed working. But the log showed a NEW failure mode from step ~56 onward: 25+ consecutive steps of pure `Tab` / `redirect-click to 'Prior Insurer'` oscillation, zero further fields filled, only interrupted when the user gave up and stopped the run.

  **Root cause, traced precisely**: the transformer's own raw pointer prediction stayed frozen at the exact same screen coordinate (`1452,540`, 'Years at Address') for 13+ consecutive steps regardless of what had just happened on screen. The existing reclick-streak redirect (`execution_reclick_streak_blind_tab_gamble`) clicked a known-good target ('Prior Insurer') every 2nd drift, but just assumed the click worked and `continue`d — the exact "assume it worked instead of verifying" mistake this project has hit and fixed multiple times already (verify-at-fill, the scroll branch). Proof it wasn't landing: this codebase's auto-fill fast path checks `state["focused_element_id"]` directly, independent of the transformer's own pointer — if the redirect click had genuinely focused 'Prior Insurer', the very next step should have shown an immediate `LLM focused-field lookup: field='Prior Insurer'` line. It never did, across 13 attempts — proof the click wasn't actually moving OS focus there, not just an unlucky transformer guess.

  **Fixed by verifying the redirect, not assuming it.** After the redirect click, `self._observe()` is called again immediately and the fresh `focused_element_id` is checked against the target's own identity (via `_attempt_key`, the project's existing stable cross-snapshot matcher). Landed → reset both the reclick streak and a new `_redirect_stall_count`, proceed normally. Didn't land → `_redirect_stall_count` accumulates (kept separate from the reclick streak, which still resets every redirect attempt so a single miss doesn't block future retries); once `_redirect_stall_count` hits `_REDIRECT_STALL_LIMIT=2`, stop repeating the identical failed maneuver and call the existing `_try_advance_tab()` instead — this tab's remaining content isn't reachable right now, so move forward rather than oscillate forever. Generic — no field/task-specific logic, reuses the same `_attempt_key` and `_try_advance_tab` mechanisms already proven elsewhere in this file.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_reclick_streak_redirect.py` extended with `TestRedirectIsVerifiedNotAssumed` (3 new tests — a redirect that lands resets both counters; one that doesn't land accumulates the stall count without advancing yet; repeated failures advance the tab). The original 3 tests (plain-Tab-on-first-drift, redirect-on-second-drift, fallback-to-Tab-when-nothing-visible) needed zero changes — the verify step only activates once a redirect target actually exists and gets clicked. Full suite 366 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report ("It's using Tab to fucking navigate. Its not finding the actual optimal view.") followed by an explicit standing instruction ("Do not use Tab to fucking navigate you piece of shit.").** This run was genuinely working (15 fields filled, 100% value accuracy, no infinite loop) — but the log still showed 'Suffix' getting Tab-skipped repeatedly (steps 11-13, then 29-30, then 32), and more generally confirmed Tab was still tolerated as a first response in two places before ever redirecting.

  **Root cause #1 — a real gap, not a false alarm**: the "combobox already attempted (known blank) — Tab, no re-click" guard (for legitimately-blank comboboxes like Suffix) was a COMPLETELY SEPARATE code path from the sibling "already-filled" reclick guard, and had **no streak tracking or escalation at all** — it just Tabbed every single time it recurred, forever, regardless of how many times in a row. This is exactly what a human watching the screen sees as "just tabbing."

  **Root cause #2 — explicit user requirement, not a bug**: both the reclick-streak guard and the low-confidence-fallback guard tolerated ONE blind Tab before escalating to a redirect (`_RECLICK_REDIRECT_LIMIT=2`, `_LOWCONF_FALLBACK_LIMIT=3`). The user's direct instruction ("do not use Tab to navigate") means even a single tolerated Tab-as-navigation-guess is unacceptable now, not just repeated ones.

  **Fixed on both counts.** (1) The combobox-already-attempted-blank branch now shares the SAME `_reclick_streak` counter and verified-redirect mechanism as the already-filled guard, instead of maintaining a second, weaker copy — one unified "stop re-visiting a dead field" escalation. (2) `_RECLICK_REDIRECT_LIMIT` dropped from 2 to 1 and `_LOWCONF_FALLBACK_LIMIT` dropped from 3 to 1 — the very first drift onto an already-handled field, or the very first low-confidence/invalid pointer, now redirects straight to Navigation Protocol's own known target via click. Tab is NOT removed globally — it still does its own legitimate job (committing a just-selected combobox value, moving off a just-filled field, and the one remaining "nothing else visible to click" fallback) — only removed as a *navigation guess*.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_reclick_streak_redirect.py` extended with `TestComboboxAlreadyAttemptedBlankAlsoEscalates` (1 new test proving the shared mechanism) and its existing tests updated for `redirect_limit=1` (the old "first drift still gets a plain Tab" test is gone — there is no such state anymore). `tests/test_lowconf_fallback_escalation.py` fully rewritten for `_LOWCONF_FALLBACK_LIMIT=1` — the old "single low-confidence guess just Tabs" tests described behavior that's now explicitly wrong; replaced with tests proving immediate escalation and an explicit "no Tab issued anywhere in the ladder" assertion. Full suite 365 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "What the fuck was that why did it go to Vehicle?"** Log evidence, decisive: the redirect-verify mechanism (from the previous fix) correctly detected that clicking 'Street Address 1' (same coordinates, `1452,844`, twice) did NOT move focus there — a genuine, field-specific click-targeting problem, not a tab-tracking or Navigation Protocol issue. But the escalation logic treated "this ONE target failed twice" as "the WHOLE TAB is exhausted" and advanced immediately — skipping a dozen other genuinely fillable Policyholder fields (City, State, ZIP, County, DL Number, DL Issuing State, DL Expiration, ...) that were never even tried. The exact same thing happened again one tab later (a second stalled redirect), compounding into a 2-tab jump straight to 'Vehicle,' abandoning the entire rest of Policyholder in the process.

  **Root cause: conflating "this specific target is unreachable" with "this tab has nothing left."** Both were true in the earlier `execution_reclick_streak_blind_tab_gamble`/verify fix's design, but they're different claims — `find_visible_empty_target()` always returns the FIRST match in element order, so if that one field happens to be unclickable, every redirect attempt re-picks the exact same broken target and guarantees the stall limit trips before anything else is ever tried.

  **Fixed by narrowing the escalation**: once a redirect target fails to stick `_REDIRECT_STALL_LIMIT` times, mark ONLY that specific field as attempted (so `find_visible_empty_target()` stops re-offering it) and search again for a DIFFERENT target before concluding the tab itself is exhausted. A different reachable target found → stay on the tab, no tab-advance. Nothing else visible either → genuine exhaustion, advance as before. Applied identically to both the already-filled reclick guard and the combobox-already-attempted-blank guard (which share the same counters since the earlier unification). Known, accepted trade-off, stated plainly: the specific unreachable field will not get filled this run — better than either looping on it forever or discarding every other real field on the same tab alongside it. `Street Address 1`'s own click-targeting failure is NOT yet root-caused — flagged as a separate open question (its exact bbox sits close to the same area previously suspected of footer/button-strip overlap in `_form_viewport_bottom`'s estimate, per an earlier, still-unconfirmed theory), not guessed at further here.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_reclick_streak_redirect.py`'s `_run_reclick_guard_verified` mirror extended with an `attempted_keys` parameter matching the real logic; new `TestStalledRedirectTriesADifferentTargetBeforeAbandoningTheTab` (2 tests — a different reachable target is tried instead of advancing; advancing still happens when genuinely nothing else is reachable). Full suite 367 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report ("It did it again, read the recent logs").** The fix above fired exactly as designed — log confirmed `"'Street Address 1' unreachable and no other target visible either — advancing"` — and STILL jumped tabs wrongly. Root cause of the fix's own gap: the "different target" search was viewport-bounded (`_form_viewport_bottom`), so it only asked "is anything else visible RIGHT NOW" — at that exact instant nothing else happened to be on screen. The very next step's scroll immediately revealed 'Street Address 2' just below the fold, which then failed the SAME way (same failure, different field), compounding into the reported jump straight to Vehicle. Fixed by searching the WHOLE tab unbounded (`1e9`, same pattern already used by the `[SCROLL-DIAG]` tracking) instead of the current viewport only — if something exists anywhere on the tab, even off-screen, stay and let Navigation Protocol's own `decide()` scroll to it naturally; only advance when truly nothing remains anywhere.

  **Also, in the same report, direct user instruction: "Don't use semantic positioning for God's sake."** Read together with the actual evidence — 'Street Address 1' AND 'Street Address 2', two different fields, click coordinates only 10px apart, BOTH failed to receive focus via a simulated click at their bbox center (confirmed twice each by re-observing) — the instruction reads as: stop trying to move focus by computing where something visually sits on screen and clicking there; use UIA's actual focus API instead. Added `_focus_element_via_uia(label)`: finds the control directly via UIA's `EditControl`/`ComboBoxControl`/`CheckBoxControl` search-by-name (no bbox, no screen coordinates at all) and calls `.SetFocus()` on it. Wired into both redirect paths (the already-filled guard and the combobox-attempted-blank guard) as the PRIMARY route, falling back to the existing coordinate click only if UIA can't find/focus the control. Doesn't touch the main transformer-driven navigate click (working well, hundreds of successful uses this session) — scoped narrowly to the two spots with actual, confirmed failure evidence.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_reclick_streak_redirect.py`'s alt-target search updated to `1e9` (unbounded) with a new test proving an off-screen (`y=5000`) alternate target is still found. `tests/test_focus_element_via_uia.py` (new, 4 tests — `uiautomation`/`win32gui` faked via `sys.modules` injection — finds and focuses a matching edit control, falls through to combobox when no edit control matches, returns `False` when nothing matches anywhere, returns `False` cleanly when UIA is unavailable). Full suite 372 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Street Address 1/2's own underlying click-targeting failure still isn't root-caused (why the click specifically misses there) — SetFocus works around it rather than explaining it; flagged, not guessed at further.

  **Same conversation, direct follow-up spec from the user**: an explicit list of 15 fields (Street Address 1 through Prior Expiry Date) that should ALL appear together as the next optimal view after Work Phone, plus a repeated, explicit instruction: "I don't want you to do trial-and-error by multiple scrolling, I want you to scroll on it once and then boom." First proposed plan: measure a pixels-per-`SmallIncrement` ratio once (25px/call, empirically constant across 8+ log samples), cache it, then fire a computed number of `SmallIncrement` calls back-to-back with no observation in between. **User pushed back with two sharp questions**: "Do you continuously scroll?" (yes, internally — still N native calls, just no visible gaps) and "why do you need to compute — don't you have the UI Accessibility Tree to just find what isn't in focus and what is?" Right call — UIA exposes exactly the needed primitive directly: `ScrollItemPattern.ScrollIntoView()`, which asks the control's own container to bring it into view however it needs to, no pixel math, no calibration, no loop.

  **Implemented the corrected design.** Added `navigation_protocol.find_scroll_target_element()`: reuses the exact same sliding-window density calculation `optimal_view_counts` already performs (refactored both to share a new `_fillable_candidates()` helper so they can never disagree about what counts), but returns the actual ELEMENT — specifically the deepest (highest bbox-center-y) member of the densest reachable cluster — instead of just a count. Added `agent.py`'s `_scroll_into_view_via_uia(label)`: looks up the control by name via UIA (shared `_find_uia_control_by_name` helper, also now used by `_focus_element_via_uia`) and calls `.GetScrollItemPattern().ScrollIntoView()` — one native call. `_scroll_form_down` now tries three routes in order: (1) compute the target element and `ScrollIntoView` it — the new primary path, exact target, no pixel guessing; (2) `_scroll_form_down_uia` (SmallIncrement) if no target was computable or ScrollIntoView isn't supported on this pane; (3) the original mouse-wheel fallback if UIA is unavailable at all.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_navigation_protocol.py`'s `TestFindScrollTargetElement` (5 new tests, including one proving it agrees with `optimal_view_counts` about which cluster is densest — they share the same candidate-gathering code now, so this is closer to a consistency check than an independent test). `tests/test_focus_element_via_uia.py` extended with `TestScrollIntoViewViaUia` (3 new tests) — caught a real test-infrastructure bug while writing them: a bare `MagicMock()` standing in for `self` auto-generates a mock for `_find_uia_control_by_name` instead of running the real refactored lookup, which would have made every test pass for the wrong reason; fixed by explicitly binding the real method via `types.MethodType`. New `tests/test_scroll_form_down_routing.py` (4 tests) proves the three-route fallback order using mocks for each route. Full suite 384 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "it navigated using Tab again... Tab or not I don't care, it only revealed one input, for fuck sake."** Read the full log carefully first, since the "Tab" framing didn't match what the log actually showed — every Tab press that run was either committing a just-selected combobox value or giving up after failed verify-at-fill retries; the "already attempted, redirecting" lines fired the SAME silent, successful `SetFocus()` calls as designed (no Tab, no click, hence no visible cursor movement — likely what read as "Tab" on screen, since nothing visibly happens before the field changes). **The real, confirmed bug**: `ScrollIntoView` never actually fired — the log showed `"UIA ScrollPattern SmallIncrement issued"`, not `"ScrollIntoView landed on..."`, meaning it silently fell back to the old one-field-at-a-time route every time, which is exactly why only "Street Address 1" (one field) got revealed instead of the intended 15-field cluster.

  Added diagnostic logging to every `_scroll_into_view_via_uia` failure branch (control not found / no `ScrollItemPattern` / exception) rather than guessing why — but also moved decisively on the most likely explanation without waiting for another live round: `ScrollItemPattern` is an OPTIONAL UIA pattern, far more commonly implemented by list/grid items than plain form fields, so it plausibly isn't implemented by this wx form's controls at all.

  **Added a second, more robust one-shot route: `_scroll_form_down_uia_percent`.** The scroll PANE's `ScrollPattern` (a different, base-level interface from `ScrollItemPattern`) is already proven working — `SmallIncrement` uses the exact same object successfully every time. `ScrollPattern.VerticalViewSize` is a REQUIRED property (not optional) reporting what fraction of total content the current viewport shows — combined with the pane's own on-screen pixel height, that gives an exact pixels-per-scroll-percent ratio from ONE read of self-reported UIA data, no calibration nudge, no scroll-and-measure cycle. Computes the target percentage and calls `SetScrollPercent()` once — a genuine absolute jump. `_scroll_form_down` now tries four routes in order: `ScrollIntoView` → `SetScrollPercent` → `SmallIncrement` → mouse-wheel.

  Refactored the shared pane-finding walk (previously only inside `_scroll_form_down_uia`) into `_find_scrollable_pane_uia`, reused by both UIA scroll routes. **Caught a real test-infrastructure bug while updating the existing SmallIncrement tests for this refactor**: they used a bare `MagicMock()` for `self`, which after the refactor silently auto-mocked `_find_scrollable_pane_uia` instead of running the real lookup — the exact same class of bug as the earlier `_focus_element_via_uia` test fix, this time in a sibling function. Fixed the same way: explicit `types.MethodType` binding via a shared `_fake_self()` helper.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_scroll_form_down_uia.py`'s `TestScrollFormDownUiaPercent` (4 new tests — computes and issues the correct absolute jump, clamps to 100%, fails cleanly when `VerticalViewSize` is unreported, fails cleanly when no scrollable pane exists) plus the `_fake_self()` binding fix for the pre-existing SmallIncrement tests. `tests/test_scroll_form_down_routing.py` extended to the new 4-route order (5 tests total). Full suite 389 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "it's still revealing them one at a time, what the fuck is going on?"** Log evidence, decisive: 17 fields filled, spanning from the top of the Policyholder tab all the way through "Country" — but ZERO `Scroll-form:` log lines anywhere in the entire run. None of the scroll machinery built tonight ever fired.

  **Root cause, genuinely new**: the reclick-streak/combobox-attempted redirect guards pick a target and call `_focus_element_via_uia` (UIA `SetFocus`) on it — and this wx form auto-scrolls whatever control receives focus into view, as its own native behavior (documented earlier this session in a completely different context, not connected to tonight's work until now). Every redirect's `SetFocus` was therefore already revealing exactly one field via that side effect — satisfying `decide()`'s `cur > 0` condition before Navigation Protocol's own explicit "scroll to the densest cluster" logic ever got a turn. The elaborate `ScrollIntoView`/`SetScrollPercent` machinery built earlier tonight was correct but irrelevant — it never got invoked, because something else was already (accidentally) handling reveals, just one field at a time.

  **Fixed by pointing the redirect guards at the same "densest cluster" target `navigation_protocol.find_scroll_target_element` computes**, instead of the nearest currently-visible field (`find_visible_empty_target`). Since `SetFocus` already auto-scrolls its target into view, aiming it at the deepest field of the next full cluster makes that same side effect reveal the whole cluster at once instead of creeping one field at a time. Applied to both the already-filled guard and the combobox-attempted-blank guard.

  **Caught a real bug in this same night's earlier work while writing the regression tests for this fix, before it ever reached a live run**: all three existing calls to `find_scroll_target_element` (the two redirect guards just added, and the original `_scroll_form_down` call from the `ScrollIntoView` work) passed `1e9` as the viewport-width parameter, intending "search on- or off-screen." But that parameter isn't a visibility bound — it's the density-window WIDTH the sliding-window algorithm itself uses. An effectively-infinite width makes any set of candidates trivially fit in one window, degenerating the whole computation into "just return the single deepest field on the entire tab," silently discarding the "fits in one real screen" concept the function exists to compute. Fixed all three call sites to use the real `self._form_viewport_bottom(state) - 8`, matching the pattern already used everywhere else in this file.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_reclick_streak_redirect.py`'s new `TestRedirectAimsAtTheDensestClusterNotJustTheNearestField` (1 test, proving the redirect now targets the deepest field of a cluster over a nearer lone field) plus updates to the existing mirror functions and one test whose expected target field changed as a direct, correct consequence of the new selection logic. Full suite 390 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "it didn't fucking finish filling up before you navigated away" / "it didn't finish actually filling in necessary tabs that were already present."** Log evidence, a genuinely new class of bug tonight: right after checking the 'Homeowner' checkbox and pressing Tab, the log reported `Field value changed: 'Male' → ''` — 'Male' was the ALREADY-FILLED 'Gender' combobox's value, several fields away from Homeowner and never touched by that action at all.

  **Root cause: `state_validator.py`'s "did a value change" check matched elements by `element_id` — the SAME unstable, scan-position-based id (`elem_{offset+count}` in `ui_observer.py`) this exact file was already fixed for once before, for a different check (`TestIgnoresChurnedIdSameContent`, the false-"done"-from-a-relabeled-status-text bug).** That earlier fix only covered the completion/error-dialog detection path; the "keyboard: did a value change" check, a few lines below it in the same function, still compared `elems_before[eid]` against `elems_after[eid]` directly. Any element-count change between the two snapshots (a scroll, a newly-revealed field, a dropdown opening — all things that happen constantly now) shifts every later id, so the two dicts can hold completely unrelated fields under the same key. 'Homeowner' being checked revealed new elements (159→163), shifting 'Gender's id, and whatever field landed in Gender's OLD slot in the new snapshot (empty) got compared against Gender's real value ('Male') as if they were the same field.

  **Fixed by matching on a stable identity (label + type) instead of position** — the same shape `agent.py`'s own `_attempt_key` already uses for this exact reason elsewhere in the codebase. This was a validator/logging-accuracy bug specifically — whether it also reflects genuine data loss (the field's real underlying value actually being cleared, versus only being misreported) isn't confirmed either way; fixing the comparison itself is correct regardless, since a validator that can misattribute value changes between arbitrary fields is unreliable on its own terms.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_state_validator_id_churn.py` extended with `TestKeyboardValueChangeIgnoresChurnedIds` (2 new tests — an unrelated field landing in the old slot is not reported as the tracked field changing; a genuine value change is still detected despite id churn). Full suite 392 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "Marital Status was not even filled up until Cell Phone but we navigated away."** Re-read the same log with fresh eyes rather than asking for a new run — confirmed: between "SSN" (step 9) and "Homeowner" (step 12), 'Marital Status', 'Occupation', 'Education Level', and 'Credit Score' never appear again anywhere in the rest of the run. Genuinely skipped, not just delayed.

  **Root cause, directly following from the same night's earlier redirect-to-deepest-cluster fix**: right after checking the 'Homeowner' checkbox (via the raw `BM_SETCHECK` Win32 message, which never moves real keyboard focus on its own), the merge logic pressed a blind Tab to move on. That Tab handed movement to WINDOWS' OWN native tab order — independent of anything Navigation Protocol decided — which triggered a SECOND, uncontrolled auto-scroll of its own. The log shows the element count dropping 163→157 at exactly this point, consistent with several fields scrolling off-screen. Stacked on top of the earlier redirect (which had already jumped the view down to the deepest field of a cluster), this second uncontrolled scroll left the shallower fields in that same cluster scrolled past — and since this module only ever scrolls DOWN, never back up, they were gone for the rest of the run. A direct, if unintended, consequence of combining "jump straight to the deepest field" with a still-blind Tab immediately afterward.

  **Fixed by redirecting to a known, currently-visible empty target (a real click) instead of gambling on native tab order** — the same principle already applied to every other Tab-as-navigation removal tonight, just one spot that had been missed (this one triggers from the checkbox-check merge branch, a different code path from the reclick-streak/combobox-attempted guards fixed earlier). Falls back to a plain Tab only if nothing is currently visible to click, matching the established pattern.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_checkbox_no_blind_tab_after_check.py` (new, 2 tests — redirects to a known visible target instead of Tab; falls back to Tab only when nothing else is visible). Full suite 394 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "It navigated away after filling in SSN" / "Marital Status was not even filled up until Cell Phone but we navigated away."** A deeper, different version of the same underlying issue. Log evidence: the redirect from 'Suffix' targeted 'Homeowner' (correctly — the deepest field of the next dense cluster, per the earlier fix), its `SetFocus` correctly revealed the whole cluster (element count 159→163, confirming Marital Status/Occupation/Education Level/Credit Score all became visible) — but the VERY NEXT step immediately filled **Homeowner itself**, not any of the newly-revealed shallower fields. None of them ever got touched.

  **Root cause: landing `SetFocus` on the deepest field to trigger the cluster's reveal ALSO makes that same deepest field the next thing that gets filled**, since the auto-fill fast path always acts on whatever `state["focused_element_id"]` currently is — and that's now the deep target, not any of the shallower fields the reveal was supposed to expose. "Reveal the cluster" and "decide what to fill next" were the same action, with no separation between them — the deepest field's job was only ever supposed to be triggering the scroll, but it ended up hogging the actual fill turn too, every time.

  **Fixed by adding a second step after confirming the redirect landed**: immediately search for the SHALLOWEST now-visible unattempted field (`find_visible_empty_target`, viewport-bounded — the cluster is now on-screen, no need to search further) and `SetFocus` onto THAT one instead, before continuing the loop. Revealing the cluster and choosing what to fill are now two separate actions instead of one field doing both jobs. If the landed field is already the shallowest (or the only) one visible, no redundant refocus happens. Applied to both the already-filled guard and the combobox-attempted-blank guard.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_reclick_streak_redirect.py`'s new `TestRefocusesShallowestFieldAfterRevealingACluster` (2 tests — refocuses to a genuinely shallower field when one exists; stays put when the landed field is already shallowest). Full suite 396 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "Check recent logs, it didn't want to navigate. What am I gonna do with you."** This run was genuinely excellent otherwise — 38 fields filled, 100% value accuracy, correctly swept through the entire Policyholder, Vehicle, Financing, and Coverage sections. But it got stuck at the very end on 'Payment Frequency': `Combobox: dropdown for 'Payment Frequency' still empty after 8 tries (3.2s) — giving up.` — the exact same message, 121 times, across nearly 4 minutes, with zero progress, until the user gave up and interrupted the run.

  **Root cause: a genuine gap, not a regression.** The combobox-click-fill branch has a sibling case a few lines above it (combobox is genuinely blank, nothing to fill) that already has a repeat-guard/blacklist mechanism (`_action_history`/`_nochange_click_pos`, an older fix from earlier this project). But THIS case — the combobox has a real wanted value, but its dropdown never renders (or its options never match) — had no streak tracking at all. It just logged a warning, pressed Escape, and looped, forever, with no memory of how many times this had just happened.

  **Fixed by adding a per-field failure counter** (`_combobox_dropdown_fail_counts`, keyed by label rather than click position, since this field's own click position doesn't drift the way the transformer's pointer does elsewhere in the file). A single failure is tolerated (could be a transient render-timing hiccup); on the second consecutive failure for the SAME field, it's marked attempted (so Navigation Protocol stops offering it) and the code redirects to a different known visible target instead of repeating the identical poll-and-give-up cycle. A successful fill resets the counter, so a field that eventually recovers doesn't carry a stale count forward. Known, accepted trade-off, stated plainly: 'Payment Frequency' itself won't get filled if its dropdown genuinely never cooperates — better than burning the rest of the run's step budget on one field while every other real target sits untouched.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_combobox_dropdown_never_renders.py` (new, 4 tests — a single failure just retries; repeated failure marks attempted and redirects; a successful fill resets the counter; different fields track failures independently). Full suite 400 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "Error on Drivers."** This run was another strong one — 38 fields, and the earlier fixes tonight (redirect-to-cluster, refocus-shallowest, checkbox-no-blind-tab) were all visibly working. But the log showed three DIFFERENT actions on three DIFFERENT fields (Date of Birth, an SR-22 checkbox, an Excluded Driver checkbox) all reporting the identical, nonsensical `Field value changed: '' → 'Female'` — 'Female' was some OTHER driver's Gender value, never touched by any of those three actions.

  **Root cause: a gap in my OWN fix from earlier tonight.** The `(label, type)` stable-key matching added to fix the element-id-churn bug doesn't account for repeated sections — the Drivers tab has Driver 1/2/3, each with its own 'Gender', 'First Name', 'Date of Birth', etc. under the identical bare label (no section prefix in that particular field). Multiple elements sharing one key meant `elems_before`'s FIRST match under that key got compared against whichever driver's field happened to be scanned in `elems_after`, cross-contaminating unrelated drivers' fields. This is the exact repeated-section collision class `agent.py`'s own `_attempt_key` already disambiguates (by rank among same-labeled elements, documented in its own docstring) — it just hadn't been carried into this file's parallel fix.

  **Fixed by mirroring that same rank-based disambiguation** — the key becomes `(label, type, rank)`, where rank is each element's position among other elements sharing that same label+type within ITS OWN snapshot (relying on `elems_before`/`elems_after` preserving original document/scan order, which they do — they're built via direct dict comprehension over the elements list). Driver 1's Gender (rank 0) now only ever matches Driver 1's Gender in the other snapshot, never Driver 2's or 3's.

  **Also flagged, not guessed at**: the offline accuracy scorer reported `Date of Birth typed='09/05/2006' expected='07/14/1978'` as a mismatch — but the log confirms the fill logic correctly used a section-prefixed lookup (`'Driver 3 Date of Birth' → '09/05/2006'`) and typed the right value into the right field. This looks like a limitation in the scoring script itself (likely comparing against a single top-level "Date of Birth" without section awareness now that multiple people have one), not a live agent bug — not investigated further tonight. Also flagged: the exact same redirect-then-refocus sequence ('Driver 3 — Excluded Driver' → 'First Name') repeated identically 3 times in a row right before the run was interrupted — possibly an emerging stuck loop, but the run ended before there was enough evidence to diagnose it; watch for on the next run.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_state_validator_id_churn.py` extended with `TestKeyboardValueChangeDisambiguatesRepeatedSections` (2 new tests — three same-labeled Gender fields don't cross-contaminate; an unrelated field's change isn't misattributed to a same-labeled sibling). Full suite 402 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "Navigation error, fix."** Log (`logs/run_task_20260809_012634.log`) showed steps 108-117: the transformer's own click prediction landed on the SAME already-checked checkbox ('Uninsured/Underinsured Motorist') 10 CONSECUTIVE steps in a row.

  **Root cause: a fourth spot using Tab-as-navigation, missed in every prior Tab-elimination sweep tonight.** The "block re-clicking an already-checked checkbox" guard (inside the click-action branch, structurally separate from the reclick-streak guard — that one only covers edit/combobox fields, never this checkbox-click guard) correctly blocked every one of the 10 clicks so the box couldn't get toggled off, but its fallback was a blind Tab every single time.

  **Fixed the same way as every other Tab-as-navigation spot tonight**: redirect to a known visible target via `find_visible_empty_target()` + `_focus_element_via_uia()` (SetFocus, no coordinate guessing), falling back to a direct click on the target's own bbox only if SetFocus fails, and to Tab only if nothing else is visible at all. Caught my own bug before shipping: first draft set `prediction = None` on a successful SetFocus redirect, then found `prediction.get("action_type")` is called unconditionally a few lines later — would have crashed on `None`. Fixed to `prediction = {"action_type": "no_op"}`, an established, already-safe action type used elsewhere in this file.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_already_checked_checkbox_no_blind_tab.py` (new, 3 tests — SetFocus redirect returns `no_op`; SetFocus failure falls back to a direct click on the target's bbox; Tab only when nothing else is visible). Full suite 405 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "Another loop error jesus fucking christ were just wasting time."** Log (`logs/run_task_20260809_014104.log`) showed steps 43-48 stuck in a tight loop on 'Homeowner' — already checked earlier in the run — with the SAME "already checked — redirecting instead of blind Tab" guard firing every single step, never progressing, until the user interrupted at step 37 actionable (48 raw). Completion 10.2% (18/176 fields), wasted-step rate 24.3%.

  **Root cause, genuinely new and deeper than last fix: `ui_observer.py` never reads checkbox toggle state at all.** `value` is only ever populated from UIA's `ValuePattern` (or, as a fallback, `TextPattern`) — but checkboxes expose `TogglePattern`, not `ValuePattern`, so `ValuePattern` always silently returned nothing for them. Every checkbox in every form, checked or not, has therefore always reported `value=""`. This was invisible before tonight because checked-state was tracked separately (`self._checked_fields`, and a direct `BM_GETCHECK` read in the click guard) — nothing needed `navigation_protocol.find_visible_empty_target()`'s "is this field's value empty" check to be checkbox-aware until THIS session's redirect guards started routing through it. With that gap: right after 'Homeowner' got checked, `find_visible_empty_target()` kept seeing it as still-empty and kept returning it as its own "next target" — the redirect's own `_focus_element_via_uia("Homeowner")` call then `SetFocus`'d right back onto the very checkbox it was trying to move away from, which made the auto-fill fast path see 'Homeowner' as freshly focused again next step, re-entering the identical guard — a tight, self-reinforcing loop with no other moving part involved.

  **Fixed at the actual data source, not by patching the guard again.** `ui_observer.py` now falls back to `ctrl.GetPattern(TogglePattern)` and reads `.ToggleState` whenever `ValuePattern` left `value` empty, setting `value="checked"` for `ToggleState.On` — universal, applies to every checkbox in any form, not a Homeowner-specific patch. Also added a belt-and-suspenders fix in `agent.py`: both checkbox-check code paths (the TYPE-intercept merge branch and the click-guard's `BM_SETCHECK` success branch) now call `self._mark_attempted()` immediately after checking, so even a redirect computed within the SAME step — before the next observation cycle would reflect the new toggle state — can't offer the same checkbox back to itself.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_ui_observer_checkbox_toggle_state.py` (new, 6 tests — checked/unchecked toggle states read correctly, `ValuePattern` still wins when present so edit/combobox fields are untouched, missing pattern and read-failure both default safely to empty, and an end-to-end check that a correctly-valued checked checkbox is excluded from `find_visible_empty_target`). `tests/test_checkbox_no_blind_tab_after_check.py` extended with `TestMarkAttemptedPreventsSameStepSelfRedirectLoop` (1 test, proving the attempted-keys layer excludes a checkbox even while its value read is still stale). Full suite 412 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "We're in a loop and we're missing certain steps. I don't even know if we're fucking using the Transformer."** This run was actually one of the better ones so far (95 actionable steps, 39 fields filled, 22.2% completion, only 4.2% wasted-step rate) — but log evidence confirmed a real, separate bug, plus one non-bug worth answering directly.

  **Confirmed bug: the generic repeat-action guard was firing on genuine progress.** `logs/latest.log`, steps 108-113: the low-confidence-pointer fallback checked a genuinely DIFFERENT checkbox each step via `BM_SETCHECK` ('Roadside Assistance', 'GAP Insurance', 'Rideshare Coverage', 'New Car Replacement', ...), then reassigned `prediction` to a boilerplate "tab to move on" afterward — since the guard fingerprints the FINAL `prediction` right before execution (not what actually happened this step), three DIFFERENT successful checks in a row all ended with the IDENTICAL trailing tab, so it saw "same action 3x" and force-Tabbed AGAIN at step 111 through its own direct `self._executor.execute()` call — which, confirmed in the log, skips the normal `[OK] ExecutionResult` / `Validator` lines every other action gets. Real, distinguishable progress was happening every step; the guard just couldn't see past the leftover bookkeeping Tab. This also explains why the end-of-run per-step summary skips certain step numbers — any step whose result never went through the normal logged path (this guard's bypass included) isn't in that list, which is very plausibly what read as "missing steps."

  **Fixed with a per-step `_real_progress_this_step` flag**, set immediately after each of the two `BM_SETCHECK` real-check code paths (the TYPE-intercept merge branch and the click-guard's success branch). The repeat-action guard now skips its fingerprint check entirely — and clears history, since real progress legitimately breaks any actual stuck streak too — whenever that flag is set, letting the normal keyboard-tab path execute afterward with its usual logging. Generalizes beyond checkboxes: any future code path that performs a real state-changing action and then leaves a boilerplate "move on" prediction behind can set the same flag.

  **Answered directly, not guessed at: "are we using the Transformer."** Confirmed via log (142+ `[TRANSFORMER]` lines, one every raw step) and via `agent.py`'s own existing architecture comment (predates tonight, not something this session touched or broke): `_HIGH_CONF = 1.01  # effectively: never skip the LLM` is deliberate — the transformer's action-TYPE head is known to collapse to always one class, so it's never trusted to decide WHAT alone; the transformer's proven strength (click_acc ~0.76) is WHERE, so OPT2 always has the transformer supply the click position while the LLM supplies the value/action choice via `_merge`. So yes, the transformer runs and drives positioning on every single step — the `Transformer Dependency 50.5%` metric only counts steps where it acted with zero LLM involvement at all, which undercounts its real, constant involvement in this hybrid design. Flagged as a known, separate, PRE-EXISTING limitation (the collapsed action-type head) worth prioritizing separately if the team wants to close the >95% target — not something guessed at or touched in this fix.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_repeat_action_guard_ignores_real_progress.py` (new, 4 tests — three different real checkbox checks in a row never trigger the guard; real progress resets an in-progress streak instead of carrying over; the guard still catches a genuine stuck loop with no progress; three different predictions with no progress never false-triggers). Full suite 416 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "Jesus fucking christ another loop in the Drivers, why do you hate me?"** Log (`logs/latest.log`) showed a genuinely new, tight loop from 02:25:44 to past 02:27:17 (35+ repeats, ~1.5s apart): every cycle, the reclick-streak guard correctly detected the transformer's pointer stuck on already-filled 'Last Name', correctly redirected to Driver 3's 'Excluded Driver' cluster, correctly refocused to the shallowest remaining target 'First Name' — and then the VERY NEXT step showed the transformer's pointer already back on 'Last Name' again, as if the refocus never happened at all. Crucially, an earlier occurrence in the SAME run (line 1259) refocused successfully to 'Gender' and real progress followed — so the mechanism itself works; something field-specific broke for 'First Name'.

  **Root cause: `_find_uia_control_by_name` (the shared lookup behind `_focus_element_via_uia`/`_scroll_into_view_via_uia`) searches by accessible Name ALONE, with no way to tell apart the SAME bare label repeated across Driver 1/2/3** (no section prefix baked into the raw UIA Name property — the exact repeated-section collision class already fixed twice elsewhere this session: `state_validator.py`'s stable-key matching, and `agent.py`'s own `_attempt_key` rank disambiguation — just never carried into this UIA-level lookup, since until tonight's cluster-revealing mechanism started running on the Drivers tab, it was only ever used for uniquely-labeled fields). `EditControl(Name="First Name")` with no disambiguation always returns the FIRST match in the UIA tree — Driver 1's, already filled — regardless of which driver's cluster the caller actually meant. `SetFocus()` succeeded every single time (which is why no error ever surfaced), just on the WRONG element: the next step's auto-fill fast path saw an already-filled field focused, took the "navigate" branch instead of filling anything, the transformer's learned pointer (frozen near 'Last Name') re-triggered the identical "already-filled, redirect" guard, and around it went — forever, since nothing ever actually got typed into Driver 3's real, empty 'First Name'.

  **Fixed by disambiguating on position, not just name.** Every existing caller already has the specific target ELEMENT (with its own bbox, from `navigation_protocol`'s own scan) in hand before calling `_focus_element_via_uia` — they were just discarding that bbox and passing the bare label through. `_find_uia_control_by_name` gained an optional `expected_bbox` parameter: when given and more than one control shares the Name, it walks every match (via UIA's own `foundIndex`, 1-based) and returns whichever one's own `BoundingRectangle` center is closest to the expected position, instead of blindly trusting tree order. Defaults to the old first-match behavior when omitted, so nothing breaks for callers without a specific position. All 7 call sites across the file (`_focus_element_via_uia`/`_scroll_into_view_via_uia`, both redirect guards' primary AND shallow-refocus targets, the combobox-dropdown-fail redirect, the checkbox-already-checked redirect, and `_scroll_form_down`'s own ScrollIntoView call) now pass their already-known target bbox through.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_focus_element_via_uia.py` extended with `TestFindUiaControlByNameDisambiguatesRepeatedLabels` (4 new tests — picks the occurrence closest to the expected position among 3 same-labeled candidates; omitting `expected_bbox` keeps the old first-match behavior; the common single-occurrence case still works; an end-to-end check that `_focus_element_via_uia` actually threads the parameter through and calls `SetFocus` on the correct occurrence, not the wrong one). `tests/test_scroll_form_down_routing.py`'s existing ScrollIntoView-success assertion updated to expect the new `expected_bbox` argument.

  **Caught a real, pre-existing test-suite bug while confirming the fix against the full suite, before it ever reached the user**: adding those 4 new tests made `tests/test_focus_element_via_uia.py`'s FINAL test the one installing fake `uiautomation`/`win32gui` modules into `sys.modules` last — and that file never restored the originals afterward. On a machine where the real packages genuinely import (this dev sandbox has both installed), that leftover fake state silently leaked into whatever test file happened to run next in the same pytest process and did its own bare `import uiautomation` — `tests/test_focus_first_empty_field_active_tab.py::test_min_y_floor_skips_the_uia_search_entirely` started failing, but ONLY when run after the UIA test file, never in isolation — a real, if latent, test-isolation gap that existed before tonight and just happened to finally get exposed by having new tests be the "last one to leak." Fixed with an autouse `pytest.fixture` in `test_focus_element_via_uia.py` that snapshots and restores both `sys.modules` entries around every test in the file, so nothing it fakes can ever escape into another file's tests again. Full suite 420 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Re-verified live, next session, direct user report: "Check recent logs, you were doing so well until the Clear All modal."** Log confirmed: right after a failed 'Payment Frequency' dropdown-open click (a field near the bottom of its tab, close to the fixed footer button row), a 'Clear All' confirmation dialog (class `#32770` — the real Windows dialog class, not a misidentified sibling window) turned up open on the very next step, with no other click logged in between.

  **Root cause: a structural gap in the destructive-button guard's own coverage, not a new kind of bug.** `_find_destructive_button_at` (added earlier this session specifically for this exact incident class) checks `_snap2` — the transformer's raw, unverified predicted click position — before it gets used as a click target. But `_snap2` is a SHARED variable, reused by TWO separate branches: the navigate branch (which got the guard) and the "CLICK on empty combobox → treat as FILL" branch a few hundred lines above it (which did not). Both branches click the identical unverified position; only one was ever protected. Given 'Payment Frequency' sits close to the footer row, this is exactly the same class of incident the guard already exists to prevent — just reachable through the one door that was never locked.

  **Fixed by extending the exact same guard to the second branch**, mirroring the navigate branch's own pattern precisely (check `_snap2` against `_find_destructive_button_at` before clicking; Tab instead and move on if it lands on any button-type element). No new mechanism — the fix already existed and was already proven correct; it just needed to cover the sibling call site that shares its risky input. `_find_destructive_button_at`'s own docstring updated to describe both call sites instead of claiming it's navigate-only.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_destructive_button_guard.py` extended with `TestComboboxOpenBranchAlsoRefusesAnyButtonClick` (2 new tests — Tabs instead of opening a dropdown that would land on 'Clear All'; still opens a normal combobox dropdown when the position is safe). Full suite 422 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "Check most recent logs, skipped most fields in Vehicle after Current Mileage, what the fuck."** Cross-checked `logs/latest.log` against `car_insurance_form_wx.py`'s own authoritative field order (`"Current Mileage" → "Annual Miles Est." → "Primary Use" → "Garaging Location" → "Purchase Date"`) rather than guessing which fields were meant — confirmed real: `'Annual Miles Est.'` never appears ANYWHERE in the entire log (not even a blank-field lookup attempt), `'Primary Use'` was targeted by the low-confidence fallback but got `no_change` (nothing happened), and `'Garaging Location'` was the scroll's own tracked target (successfully revealed) but the very next action landed on `'Purchase Date'` instead — three consecutive fields skipped between two real fills. Run was user-interrupted at step 54, so this is a partial trace, not a full picture.

  **No fix shipped — added a targeted diagnostic instead of guessing a root cause the log doesn't actually support.** The one concrete fact the log gives: right where `'Annual Miles Est.'` should sit by field order, a plain navigate click executed and the validator confirmed `"Focus moved after click"` — but the NEXT step never logged `"LLM focused-field lookup"` for it, meaning the OPT2 fast path's `_t_is_type` check (whichever field WAS focused, run through `editcontrol/combobox/checkbox` type + empty + not-already-attempted) evaluated False. Two live-plausible explanations, genuinely indistinguishable from this log alone: (a) the click didn't actually land on `'Annual Miles Est.'`'s real input control (missed it, hit a label or the wrong nearby widget), or (b) `focused_element_id` didn't match anything in the freshly re-scanned `state["elements"]` — the same element-id-churn bug class already fixed twice this session for `state_validator.py` and `agent.py`'s own `_attempt_key`, just possibly resurfacing in this THIRD place (the plain focused-element lookup at the top of the OPT2 branch). Guessing between these without evidence risks the exact mistake this project's whole methodology exists to avoid.

  Added `[FOCUS-DIAG]` logging (no behavior change): whenever something was focused after a navigate click but doesn't get treated as fillable (and it isn't a checkbox, which already has its own explained reason via the TogglePattern fix from earlier tonight), logs either `"focused_element_id not found among N scanned elements"` (the churn signature) or the found element's own type/value/attempted flags — so the next live run gives a direct, cheap answer instead of another guess. Regression tests: `tests/test_focus_diagnostic_logging.py` (new, 6 tests — mirrors the exact condition for both the id-churn and found-but-excluded cases, confirms it stays silent for the normal successful path, for nothing-focused, and for the already-explained checkbox case). Full suite 428 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. **Needs one more live run before the real fix can be identified** — matches this project's own established pattern (used successfully several times earlier tonight) of diagnosing before guessing when the log genuinely doesn't say enough.

  **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "It submitted but were we missing any fields? We're improving."** The run genuinely was better — cleaner overall, and the `[FOCUS-DIAG]` diagnostic added moments earlier fired constantly but showed EVERY single hit was the normal, benign case (an already-correctly-filled field being passed over, real value present, `already_attempted=True`) — confirming the diagnostic itself works correctly and isn't spamming false alarms. But cross-checking specifically for `'Annual Miles Est.'` (the field flagged missing last time) found it **still** never appears anywhere in this log either — and this time `[FOCUS-DIAG]` caught the mechanism directly: a plain navigate click aimed at it (its own bbox matched the click position) resulted in `[FOCUS-DIAG] focused 'Submit' type='buttoncontrol'` on the very next step — real focus landed on the Submit button instead, not the intended field.

  **Root cause, finally confirmed with direct evidence, not guessed.** `_record_attempt()` — the function that marks a field "attempted" so it's never offered as a target again — was always called with `state`, the STALE observation taken BEFORE the click executed, used only to build the prediction in the first place. For a click action, it looks up `self._elem_at(state, click_position)` — which, by construction, ALWAYS matches whatever element the click was AIMED at (since that position was itself derived by snapping to a candidate element in that same `state`). It never once checked what actually happened. The caller already takes a fresh `state_after` observation for validation a few lines below — it just was never threaded through to this call. So when the real click, for whatever OS/UIA-level reason, landed on the wrong control, the function still confidently marked the ORIGINALLY-INTENDED field "attempted" — permanently and silently excluding a field that was never actually focused or typed into. Same "verify what actually happened instead of assuming the intended target was hit" lesson this project has hit and fixed multiple times before (verify-at-fill, the redirect-verify fix), just never applied to this specific piece of bookkeeping until now.

  **Fixed by threading `state_after` through and preferring its verified `focused_element_id` for CLICK actions.** Falls back to the old position-based lookup only when `state_after` has nothing focused or wasn't provided (fully backward compatible). Deliberately left the KEYBOARD/type branch unchanged — a type action can legitimately trail a commit keystroke within the same step (e.g. a combobox selection), which could leave `state_after` focused on the NEXT field rather than the one just typed into, so the pre-action `state`'s focused element stays the more reliable signal there; this fix is scoped exactly to where the evidence pointed, not generalized further than the log actually supports.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_record_attempt_verifies_actual_focus.py` (new, 6 tests — marks the actually-focused element instead of the intended click target when they disagree, reproducing the exact 'Annual Miles Est.'/'Submit' scenario; the normal case where the click lands correctly is unaffected; both fallback paths when `state_after` has nothing useful; keyboard actions confirmed unaffected even when `state_after` shows a different field focused). Full suite 433 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Found and fixed in the SAME session, direct user request ("Fix more please")** — a second, related gap in the same run's log, caught by cross-checking the actual ground-truth data (`data_entry_tasks/data_entry_intake.txt`) rather than trusting the run's own summary: `'Primary Use'` has a real value (`'Commute'`) for the record being filled, but it also never got typed in, and the log showed `"combobox 'Primary Use' already attempted (known blank) — redirecting"` — yet `"LLM focused-field lookup: field='Primary Use'"` never appears anywhere in the entire run, meaning it was never actually looked up at all.

  **Root cause: the "combobox already confirmed blank" skip-check read the wrong set.** It checked `self._attempted_keys` — a GENERAL set populated by ANY click or keyboard action, including a bare low-confidence-fallback navigate click that happened to land on `'Primary Use'` purely while passing through, with no lookup ever attempted. The check's own comment claimed "already confirmed blank earlier this record," but `_attempted_keys` only ever means "something touched this element," not "we looked it up and it's genuinely empty" — exactly the distinction `self._leave_blank_keys` already exists to make elsewhere in this file (the OPT2 fill-gate's own `_fe2_confirmed_blank` check), just never applied to this specific combobox skip-check.

  **Fixed by pointing the skip-check at `self._leave_blank_keys` instead**, and by having the genuine "combobox %r — no value, Tab" branch (where `self._lookup_field` is actually called and actually comes back empty) add to that set alongside its existing `_mark_attempted()` call. Verified this doesn't reintroduce the ORIGINAL incident this check was built for ('Suffix', a field genuinely blank in the record, which used to repeat the full click-open-check-escape-tab dance 4 times before this check existed) — 'Suffix' still goes through the real lookup on its first visit, still lands in `_leave_blank_keys`, and is still correctly skipped on every visit after. Only a field that was never actually looked up now correctly gets a real fill attempt instead of being silently written off.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_combobox_blank_skip_requires_real_lookup.py` (new, 3 tests — a field merely touched by a stray click still gets a real fill attempt; a field genuinely looked up and confirmed empty still skips correctly; an untouched field with no entry at all is never skipped). Full suite 436 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.

  **Found and fixed in the SAME session, direct user report: "Agent is stuck please check most recent logs."** Log showed a genuinely severe run — 54.1% wasted-step rate (46 no_change / 85 actionable), the worst of the night. The tell: `[FOCUS-DIAG]` showed `'ZIP Code'` as the focused element for 6+ CONSECUTIVE steps in a row, while every one of those steps logged `"[OPT2] 1 low-confidence fallbacks in a row — Navigation Protocol taking over: known target 'Occupation'"`, clicked, and got `"Validator → no_change"` — every single time, with the streak counter resetting to 0 on each "successful" target-find regardless of whether the click actually landed.

  **Root cause: this was the ONE sibling redirect guard in the file that never received the verify-and-escalate upgrade the others got earlier this session.** The reclick-streak guard, the combobox-attempted guard, and the checkbox-already-checked guard all use UIA `SetFocus` (via `_focus_element_via_uia`) and then re-observe to confirm focus genuinely landed before trusting it. This low-confidence-fallback branch — structurally identical in purpose, just triggered by low pointer confidence instead of a repeated drift — still fell through to a raw, unverified coordinate click on the shared execution pipeline below. Because "found a target" and "the click worked" were treated as the same thing, a click that silently failed to move focus (exactly the same class of click-targeting failure already found and worked around for Street Address 1/2, 'Annual Miles Est.', and others) could repeat identically forever with nothing ever noticing.

  **Fixed by extending the exact same proven pattern, not inventing a new one.** `_focus_element_via_uia(label, expected_bbox=...)` first, falling back to a coordinate click only if SetFocus fails; re-observe and verify via `_attempt_key` match; on a stall, escalate via the same shared `_redirect_stall_count`/`_REDIRECT_STALL_LIMIT` counters the sibling guards already use, marking the specific unreachable target attempted so it stops being re-offered.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_lowconf_fallback_escalation.py` extended with `TestLowConfFallbackRedirectIsVerifiedNotAssumed` (4 new tests — prefers SetFocus over a raw click; falls back to a coordinate click when SetFocus fails; reproduces the exact live 'ZIP Code'/'Occupation' stall; repeated stalls mark the target attempted).   **Re-verified live immediately after, direct user report: "Old problems it skipped alot of Policyholder tabs, what the fuck are we doing?"** Log showed the user interrupting a run right after watching it jump from filling `'SSN'`/`'Suffix'` straight to `'DL Number'`. Since this module only ever scrolls DOWN, never back up (a limitation flagged explicitly at least twice earlier this session), those twelve fields were permanently gone for the rest of the run — real, silent data loss, not just a slow reveal.

- [x] `execution_navigate_fill_collapse` — **Direct user request, same session: "instead of a lookup every time for the different types of input fields can we look up alot at once to stop wasting steps."** Checked the actual mechanism before implementing anything: value lookups were already batched — one parse of all 176 fields at the start of each record (`Record cache refreshed: 176 fields`), reused via a cache for the rest of the run (`LLM call skipped — direct lookup already answered`, `cache_size=176`, dominating every log tonight). Lookups were never the cost. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` what "wasting steps" actually meant: nearly every field in tonight's logs took **two** separate steps — one step for a plain navigate click to focus the field, a second step for the next iteration's own top-of-loop check to notice it's now focused-and-empty and actually type into it.

  **Implemented a narrowly-scoped collapse**, not a broad restructuring of the loop. Right after a plain navigate click's post-action `state_after` is observed (a state the code already takes for validation — nothing new fetched), check whether the newly-focused element is an empty `editcontrol`/`input` with a known record value; if so, click its own bbox and type the value immediately, in the SAME step, instead of waiting for the next iteration to notice. Reassigning `prediction` to a `keyboard`/`text` shape and re-observing `state_after` means the existing verify-at-fill retry logic, `StateValidator`, and `_record_attempt` all run exactly as they already do for a normal type action — no separate verification path needed.

  Deliberately scoped narrow, not "collapse everything": only fires for a plain navigate click (`_is_plain_navigate_click`), never a redirect/combobox-open/checkbox click — those already have their own correct, more involved multi-step mechanics and forcing them through this same shortcut risked conflicting with already-proven-correct flows. Only `editcontrol`/`input` — combobox fills are their own open-dropdown-then-select sequence, not a simple collapse candidate. Only when a real value is found — a genuinely blank field is left for the next step's own established blank-handling logic (`_leave_blank_keys`, the fix earlier this session) rather than duplicating that logic here. Also fixed a subtlety while implementing: `_record_attempt()`'s keyboard branch reads the focused element from the state captured *before* the step began (deliberately, for the normal case — see that function's own docstring from earlier tonight) — but here the target field only became focused *during* this same step, so that lookup would find the wrong (previous) field. Marks the real target directly instead of relying on that downstream call for this one case.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_navigate_fill_collapse.py` (new, 7 tests — collapses and types immediately when a new empty field with a known value is focused; does NOT collapse for a non-plain-navigate click, a combobox field, a genuinely blank field, a no-op click where focus never moved, an already-typed field, or an already-confirmed-blank field). Full suite 448 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures.   **Fixed by adding the matching forward guard**: a tab-strip click landing more than one tab ahead of the current one now redirects to the immediately NEXT tab instead of trusting the skip — preserving the original fix's actual intent (go where the model clicked, don't blindly advance one-at-a-time) for the legitimate cases (the immediately-next tab, or re-clicking the current tab) while blocking the specific gap neither guard covered.
