- [x] `treetask_3d_readability_pass` — **2026-08-17, direct request** ("I want to see the nodes floating freely and actually on screen not just throwaways and clustered. I actually want them to have full views, seeing the roundness of it all. Lastly, when I click on one I can actually read the details on what they're about."). Important context check done before touching anything: the 3D scene wasn't missing — `treetask/index.html` already had a real, hand-rolled perspective-projection engine (actual (x,y,z) node positions, drag-to-orbit, wheel/pinch zoom, click-to-focus), not a flat 2D diagram. Confirmed directly with the user that nothing was technically broken; the ask was to improve an existing, working 3D view, not build one from scratch — re-classified the work down from an initial "architectural/new subsystem" assumption once that was clear.

  Three changes, all inside `buildGeometry()`/`draw()`/the interaction handlers in the same file: (1) **Node spread widened substantially** — task nodes used to fan out over a narrow ~23-69° arc at nearly the same distance from their hub (24-32 units), reading as one tight clump; fan spread, elevation spread, and per-node distance from the hub (now 34-108 units, both azimuth AND elevation vary) were all widened, and hub-to-trunk distance increased to give each phase's now-larger node cloud room without colliding with its neighbors. (2) **Real sphere shading** — nodes were flat `ctx.arc()` strokes/fills with no shading at all; added a `sphereFill()` helper (radial gradient, bright spot offset toward one corner fading to a darker rim) applied to done/locked/dormant nodes so they read as round objects with actual volume, not flat dots — architectural-blocker diamonds kept their distinct flat-stroke shape on purpose, that signal wasn't asked to change. Base node radius also increased (2.2-3.2 → 3.4-4.6) since most nodes rendered as 2-3px specks at the old size.

  (3) **Hover tooltip removed entirely, replaced with a click-opened detail panel.** Direct, explicit rejection of hover-based reading, stated twice across this and an earlier turn. The old `.tooltip` was a fixed, `pointer-events:none` box capped under 300px wide at 11.5-12.5px text, disappearing the instant the pointer moved — never going to work for this project's actual description lengths (some run to several hundred words). Replaced with `.detail-panel`, a right-docked drawer (`translateX` slide, ~430px wide, full height, scrollable) that opens on a click (task node OR hub — a hub click still also toggles its collapse, same as before) and stays open until closed (× button, Escape, or a click that hits empty canvas). Hover now purely drives the existing glow/highlight boost already built into the node-drawing code — no text, no popup — matching "I don't want to view their info simply by hovering" precisely. The Legend panel (top-right, same corner the drawer opens from) fades out while the drawer is open rather than letting the two overlap.

  Verified: extracted and `node --check`'d the full inline `<script>` block, brace-balance-checked the `<style>` block (79/79), relaunched in a real browser to confirm layout and behavior. No changes to `PHASES`, the DEVELOPERS.md sync logic, prerequisite/lock computation, or collapse persistence — render/interaction layer only.

  **Follow-up, same session, direct request**: "Can we like have a separate dedicated view for the nodes that were zooming in on? It looks dirty if we see everything else (the other branches)." Even with the spread/shading fixes above, zooming into one node still rendered the entire tree behind it — every other phase's node cloud competing for attention. Added a `focusedPhase` variable, set to the clicked node/hub's own phase whenever the detail panel opens (and cleared when it closes — the two states are tied together on purpose, not tracked separately) and checked inside `draw()`'s item-building loop: the focused phase renders exactly as before; every other phase's task nodes and task edges are dropped from the frame entirely (that's the actual clutter — dozens of small dots, not the handful of hub markers); other hubs stay as small, unlabeled, near-invisible markers (alpha 0.12) so the rest of the tree's existence is still legible without competing for attention; the root and trunk are untouched, always visible as the shared anchor. A dim hub stays clickable — tapping one jumps focus straight to that branch instead of requiring a return to the full view first. "Reset view" now also calls `closeDetailPanel()`, clearing focus alongside the camera reset.

  Asked directly whether TDD should apply here per the project's own testing skill, since this repo genuinely has no JS test framework for this file (confirmed: `package.json` only defines `start`/`build`); user confirmed skipping TDD for this change specifically rather than standing up a framework for one visual toggle — same manual-verification precedent as every other Task Tree/Electron UI change this session. Verified via `node --check` on the extracted script and a live browser check of focus mode entering/exiting correctly.

  **Second follow-up, same session**: "The navigation is still so damn bad." Narrowed down with a direct question rather than guessing which of several plausible causes it was — confirmed the complaint was "hard to tell where you are / get back," not framing or drag/zoom responsiveness. Found two real, concrete causes reading the camera code, not assumed: (1) `targetPoint` (the camera's recenter pivot, set on every click-to-focus and on Reset) was used directly inside `rotate()` with zero easing — a click or Reset snapped the whole view to a new pivot instantly, in contrast to `zoom`, which already eased smoothly toward `targetZoom`. (2) Reset's `rotY`/`rotX` also snapped directly to `0.6`/`0.18` with no transition at all. Both are legitimate, specific sources of disorientation — a hard cut breaks the sense of continuous motion between "where you were" and "where you landed."

  Fixed both without touching manual drag/auto-rotate, which need to stay instant/1:1 with input, not eased: added `camPoint`, a separate value `rotate()` now actually reads, eased toward `targetPoint` every frame (`camPoint += (targetPoint-camPoint)*0.12`) — camera recentering now happens as a followable slide, not a teleport. Reset's rotation snap became a short, explicit 500ms tween (`resetAnim`, eased with the existing `easeOutCubic()` helper already used for the tree's growth-in animation) — tracked as a separate one-shot animation rather than folding into the continuous per-frame ease, specifically so it can't fight live dragging or auto-rotate's own ongoing `rotY` increments. A manual drag starting mid-tween now cancels it (`cancelResetAnim()`, called alongside the existing `pauseAutoRotate()` in the pointerdown handler) so taking manual control always wins cleanly.

  Also added the two things the complaint named directly: a "you are here" line (`📍 Full tree` / `📍 <phase name>`) in the altimeter panel, updated the moment the detail panel opens/closes — a constant textual anchor independent of wherever the camera happens to be pointing — and a "← Back to full tree" pill, fixed near the top of the screen, visible only while a branch is focused. Both Reset view and the new pill call one shared `resetView()` rather than duplicating the reset logic in two places that could drift out of sync.

  Verified: brace-balance check on the style block (84/84), `node --check` on the extracted script, relaunched in a real browser to confirm the eased recenter/reset motion, breadcrumb text, and back-pill show/hide all behave correctly.

  **Third follow-up, same session**: "They nodes are being cut off from the frame. I actually want to see them float and be interactable through proper viewing, please fix." A real, direct consequence of two earlier changes in this same node combined: widening node spread (up to 108 units from a hub, first change of this node) plus click-to-focus zooming to a fixed `targetZoom = 6` centered on the exact clicked point (unchanged since before this node's work) — siblings far from that one point, within the same now-much-wider branch, landed outside the visible canvas at that zoom level. The old fixed-6-centered-on-one-point approach was tuned for the pre-widen spread (24-32 units); it was never updated when the spread changed.

  Fixed by framing the camera on the whole branch instead of one point. `buildGeometry()` now tracks `hub.maxReach` per phase (the farthest any of its own task nodes sits from the hub) and gives each node a direct `hub` reference. New `frameOnPhase(hub)` sets `targetPoint` to the hub's own position (not the individual clicked node) and computes `targetZoom` from `hub.maxReach` against the actually-available screen space (`window.innerWidth` minus room for the ~430px detail panel, `window.innerHeight`) — a heuristic inverse of `project()`'s own scale formula, clamped to `[1.4, 7]`. Both a task-node click and a hub click now call this (previously only task clicks moved the camera at all); the whole focused branch — hub plus every sibling — lands framed together, with the clicked item just being one of the now-visible siblings rather than the sole thing guaranteed to be on screen.

  Verified: `node --check` on the extracted script, plus a numeric sanity check of the fit formula across the real min/max `maxReach` range (34 to 108 units) before trusting it live — produces roughly zoom 6.3 for a tightly-clustered small phase and roughly zoom 2.3 for the widest one, both comfortably within the clamp and consistent with the old fixed-6 behavior at the small end. Relaunched in a real browser.

  **Fourth follow-up, same session — a real methodology change, not just another fix.** User: "The navigation is still so damn bad" / "I actually want to see them float and be interactable through proper viewing, please fix." Three rounds of fixes in this node (spread/shading, focus mode, camera easing + the `frameOnPhase` framing math) had each addressed a real, verified-on-paper issue, but the reported problem kept coming back in a new shape — the exact "3+ fixes, each revealing a new problem elsewhere" pattern the project's own debugging discipline treats as a signal to stop guessing, not try a fourth patch. The actual root cause of every round's fixes: **none of them were ever visually confirmed.** This file has no build step and no browser automation was available directly — every "fix" was code-read + hand-worked perspective math, verified by `node --check` and asking the user to look. Said so directly rather than proposing a fifth guess.

  Asked the user how to get real visual evidence; while confirming no live browser tool existed for me, found and verified one instead: this repo already has a working Playwright + Chromium install (proven earlier for Scope #2's mock-portal automation) — `python -c "from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright; ... p.chromium.launch(headless=True)"` launches cleanly in this environment. Wrote a throwaway script (`tt_screenshot.py`, scratchpad only, not committed) that opens `treetask/index.html` via a real `file://` URL, reads `screenNodes` directly via `page.evaluate()` to find a real task node's actual projected screen position, clicks it with `page.mouse.click()`, waits for the eased camera to settle, and screenshots the result. This is a genuine capability upgrade for this file specifically, not just this bug: future Task Tree UI changes can be screenshot-verified directly instead of guessed at.

  The first real screenshot immediately showed the actual bug, which no amount of hand math had found: a focused branch's own sibling nodes ran off the right edge (under the detail panel) and the bottom edge of the screen. Root cause, visible directly rather than inferred: `project()`'s screen anchor was a fixed `(w*0.5, h*0.86)` regardless of focus state — correct for the full, unfocused tree (the ground-plane-grows-up framing wants a low anchor), but never adjusted for (a) the ~430px detail panel eating the right side of the window, or (b) the fact that a focused branch has no "ground" to anchor low against, it just needs centering. Fixed directly: `panelW` (matching the panel's own CSS `min(430px, 92vw)`) is now excluded from the available width whenever `focusedPhase` is set, and `groundY` uses `h*0.52` (centered) instead of `h*0.86` (near the bottom) while focused, reverting to the original `h*0.86` for the unfocused overview (unchanged, verified identical in the before/after screenshots).

  Re-ran the same screenshot script after the fix: every sibling node in the focused branch now renders fully inside the visible area, nothing under the panel or past the bottom edge. Also screenshotted the "← Back to full tree" flow to confirm it still returns to an identical, unaffected overview. One new, real issue found via this same evidence, not yet fixed: the "COMPLETE INTERN" root label can land overlapping the fixed header text once focused, and the full overview itself (unrelated to this round's fix) still shows real overlap between adjacent phases' node clouds — both flagged to the user as separate, next work rather than silently bundled into this fix.

  **Fifth follow-up, same session — investigating both flagged issues with the new screenshot tool, per direct request ("Do it").** Extended the throwaway script into a small suite: a full-overview screenshot plus screenshots after clicking three different hubs spread across the tree (Scalability, UI/UX, Learning), to see whether the root-label-overlap was a one-off or consistent.

  Consistent across all three, but not quite what was expected: the *full* "COMPLETE INTERN" hex+label rarely showed at all once focused (usually off past the top edge) — what actually overlapped the header every time was a second, separate element: the orange "current-depth ring" (a glowing progress marker drawn along the trunk at a fixed world height, unrelated to whichever hub is focused). Fixed both root and ring the same way, consistent with the existing focus-mode pattern of dimming anything that isn't the focused branch: the root's hex drops to alpha 0.18 and its text labels are skipped entirely while `focusedPhase` is set (the altimeter panel's own "you are here" line already covers the anchor role those labels were serving); the depth-ring drops to alpha 0.12 and loses its glow. Re-screenshotted all three hubs — header collision gone in every case, only a thin, non-glowing trunk line passes behind the header text now, which reads as acceptable ambient context rather than clutter.

  Second issue — the overview's own node-cloud overlap between phases — turned out to be a real, deeper problem than the first, not something already fixed by the `project()` change: the default camera (`zoom=1`, fixed `rotY`/`rotX`) was tuned before this session's node-spread widening and no longer fit. Tested a `DEFAULT_ZOOM` reduction (1 → 0.66 → 0.55) with real screenshots each step — 0.55 was the point where nothing ran off any edge (`groundY` also lowered slightly, 0.86 → 0.80, for the unfocused case only). But a second click-test with the exact screen coordinates the script itself computed landed on the *wrong* hub twice in a row (aiming for "UI/UX," hitting "Execution and Integration"; aiming for "Learning," hitting "Data Entry Form Filling") — real, reproduced evidence, not assumed, that low zoom for edge-safety and enough on-screen separation to click precisely are in direct tension.

  Quantified it before proposing anything: read `GEO.hubs.map(h => project(h.p))` directly to measure the actual on-screen spread of all 11 hubs, then swept zoom values (0.55/0.85/1.1/1.3) via `page.evaluate()` overriding the live camera state, re-measuring spread and re-screenshotting each one. Confirmed with data, not guesswork: spread grows from 112×138px at zoom 0.55 to 265×327px at zoom 1.3, but 1.3's screenshot shows the same bottom-clustered phases running off the edge again — no zoom value satisfies both constraints simultaneously with the current camera model. This is a real trade-off in the geometry/camera design, not a bug with a parameter fix — said so directly and asked the user how to proceed rather than trying a sixth zoom value.

  Confirmed decision: make the Legend panel's phase list (top-right, already lists all 11 phases by name) a reliable alternative navigation path instead of continuing to fight 3D click precision. `.phase-row`'s click handler now also finds its matching `GEO.hubs` entry and calls `openDetailPanel({hub: hubEntry})` + `frameOnPhase(hubEntry)` alongside the existing `toggleCollapse(p)` — each row is a real, unambiguous DOM element, so this sidesteps the on-screen-proximity problem entirely rather than solving it. Verified directly: clicked the "UI/UX" Legend row (the exact phase a 3D click had just missed) via `page.click("text=UI/UX")` and screenshotted — correct phase, correct detail panel content, correctly framed, no ambiguity.

  Verified: `node --check` after every edit in this round, real screenshots (not math) confirming each fix before moving to the next, one variable changed at a time. `DEFAULT_ZOOM`/Reset's target zoom now share one constant so they can't drift apart, matching the same discipline already used for `REST_ROT_Y`/`REST_ROT_X`.

  **Sixth follow-up, same session, direct request** ("add a 2D view model cause you're really kicking my ass"). Added a real fallback that sidesteps the whole 3D-camera problem class in one move, rather than another round of camera fixes: a `#flatView` — a plain, scrollable, expandable list (phases as collapsible headers with a progress bar, tasks as rows underneath) built once from the same `PHASES` data, with zero positioning/projection math. Reuses the exact same `openDetailPanel()`/`toggleCollapse()`/`frameOnPhase()` the 3D and Legend views already call, so there's one detail-reading UI and one collapse-state source of truth, not three independently-maintained ones — `updatePhaseRow()` extended to also sync a phase's flat-view element (`p._fvPhaseEl`) alongside its existing Legend-row sync. A `btnToggleView` button switches `canvas`/`#flatView` visibility and stops rescheduling `requestAnimationFrame(draw)` while in 2D mode (resumed on switching back) so a hidden canvas doesn't keep re-rendering for nothing.

  Verified with the same real-screenshot-and-interaction method as every other round in this node, not assumed: toggled to 2D and confirmed via direct property checks that `canvas.hidden===true` and `#flatView.hidden===false` (not just visual inspection); clicked a phase header and confirmed its own tasks actually hid (`5 tasks -> 0 visible`); clicked a task row and confirmed the detail panel opened with the exact right title read back from the DOM; toggled back to 3D and confirmed the canvas resumed and rendered correctly. One real bug caught this way, not by eyeballing: the flat view's top padding (120px) wasn't enough to clear the fixed header, so the first phase card visibly overlapped the intro paragraph in the very first screenshot — fixed to 230px and re-verified clean.

  **Direct correction, same session, immediately after**: *"What? You didn't create a 2D view of the tree. You made tables and shit, I want a 2D Tree."* Fair — the build above was an accordion list, not a tree diagram. Rebuilt `#flatView` for real: a root node, one row per phase branching off it, each phase's own tasks branching further right off that row — root anchored at the left edge, phases stacked vertically so an expanded phase's tasks can never collide with a neighboring phase regardless of task count, with no packing algorithm needed to prevent it. Nodes stayed real DOM buttons (exact clicks, no screen-projection picking the 3D view needs); an SVG layer draws root→hub and hub→task connector lines from each node's REAL measured position (`getBoundingClientRect()`, read after layout) rather than hand-computed coordinates, so the lines can never drift out of sync with what's actually rendered. `drawTreeLines()` re-runs on every collapse toggle and on window resize.

  The first real screenshot immediately caught two bugs that no amount of code-reading would have found: (1) **the 3D canvas was still visible underneath the tree.** `canvas.hidden` wasn't working at all — root-caused to a genuine CSS specificity tie: `canvas{ display:block }` (an author-stylesheet element-type rule) beats the browser's own `[hidden]{ display:none }` rule at equal specificity (both are single simple selectors; the author rule wins the tie), so setting `.hidden = true` never actually hid anything. Fixed with an explicit `canvas[hidden]{ display:none }` override (higher specificity — element + attribute selector beats element alone), verified via `getComputedStyle` returning `'none'`, not just a visual check. (2) **Tree rows extended underneath the fixed Legend (top-right) and Altimeter (bottom-left) panels** — both `position:fixed`, so they cover whatever scrolls under their screen position at *any* scroll offset, not just once. Fixed the same way as the header-overlap bug earlier in this same node: widen the container's left/right padding so tree content never starts in that x-range at all, instead of fighting z-index.

  Re-verified after both fixes, real evidence each time: `getComputedStyle` confirms `display: none` on the canvas while in 2D mode; a real screenshot shows zero panel overlap both at the top of the page and after scrolling 500px down; a task click still opens the correct detail panel (title read back from the DOM, not assumed); collapsing a phase drops its visible task count to 0 and the total connector-line count drops by exactly the right amount (165 → 163 for 2 newly-hidden tasks) — confirmed with real counts, not assumed correct.

  **Direct correction, same session, immediately after that**: *"Not like that. I still want a top-bottom layout."* The left-to-right, root-on-the-left build above was a legitimate tree, just the wrong orientation. Rebuilt again: root at the top, phases in a horizontal row directly below it, each phase's own tasks stacked in a vertical column beneath that phase — a real org-chart shape. Each phase keeps a fixed-width column (216px) so an expanded phase's tasks stack straight down inside it and can never collide with a neighbor's column regardless of task count, with no packing algorithm needed. With 11 phases at a readable column width the diagram runs wider than most viewports on purpose — `.flat-view` scrolls both directions rather than cramming 11 branches into one screen width, the same tradeoff a real large org chart or family tree makes. `drawTreeEdge()`'s bezier curve direction flipped from horizontal (`midX`-based, for the old left-to-right flow) to vertical (`midY`-based), since every connection — root→hub and hub→task alike — now flows top-to-bottom.

  First real screenshot of the rebuild caught a real bug immediately, not by inspection: the root node was centered via `margin:0 auto` against its parent's *full* content width — correct instinct for a narrower view, but with 11 columns that parent is ~2662px wide, so the root ended up centered around x≈1331, well past the right edge of a normal viewport and invisible without first scrolling to find it. Fixed by left-aligning the root instead of centering it over content wider than any realistic screen — confirmed via a direct `getBoundingClientRect()` check that it's actually within the viewport at `scrollLeft=0`, not just visually eyeballed. Also reverted the earlier 280px anti-overlap padding trick for this layout specifically: it only ever worked when content was narrower than the viewport and could be centered inside the padding; once content needs horizontal scrolling, the fixed Legend/Altimeter panels *will* cover whatever tree content happens to scroll under their fixed screen position at some point, the same way they already float over the 3D scene during rotation — accepted as normal floating-chrome behavior rather than chased further.

  Re-verified the full interaction set one more time after the top-down rebuild, real checks not assumptions: task click still opens the correct detail panel (title read from the DOM); collapsing a phase drops its column's visible task count from 2 to 0 and the connector-line count drops from 165 to 163 in lockstep; toggling back to 3D restores `canvas` to `display: block`.

  **Fourth follow-up on the 2D view, same session, direct request**: *"Center the peak, space it out, zoom out, and also organize into subbranches, not everything has to be a leaf."* Four real, distinct changes:

  1. **Center the peak** — the previous fix (left-aligning the root) solved "invisible off-screen" but left it un-centered. Switched to `position:sticky; left:0; right:0; margin:0 auto` — sticky with both offsets pins the root to the *visible viewport*, not the full scrollable document, so `margin:auto` centers it within whatever's currently on screen and it stays centered at any horizontal scroll position, instead of drifting relative to 11 columns' worth of content the way plain `margin:auto` centering did before.

  2. **Space it out** — column gap widened 26px→34px, task gap 7px→9px, hub-to-tasks padding 16px→20px.

  3. **Zoom out** — column width 216px→186px, hub/task font sizes and padding all reduced ~10-15%, fitting noticeably more of the tree in one screen without scrolling. Deliberately NOT a CSS `transform:scale()` — `drawTreeLines()` reads `getBoundingClientRect()`, which already reflects any transform applied to an ancestor; scaling the container would scale those already-scaled measurements a second time when writing them into the SVG's own (pre-transform) coordinate space, silently misaligning every line. Real plain-CSS sizing sidesteps that risk entirely for a purely cosmetic effect.

  4. **Sub-branches** — the real structural piece. Checked the data first rather than inventing a taxonomy: `requires` (the existing prerequisite field) is far too sparse to use (Execution has 74 items, only 6 carry `requires`). Found a real signal instead already present in how every id in this file has been named all project: `execution_combobox_toggle_fix`, `execution_combobox_leave_blank_infinite_loop`, etc. share a real topic keyword as their id's second underscore-segment. `groupPhaseItems()` buckets a phase's items by that keyword mechanically (not hand-curated); any keyword shared by 3+ items becomes a collapsible sub-branch, anything with 1-2 items stays a direct leaf exactly as before — not worth a branch for that few. Sub-branches default collapsed (their own new `subgroupCollapsed` state map, separate from the existing per-phase `_collapsed` flag) so the initial view is genuinely more compact, which was the actual point. `drawTreeLines()` extended to a third tier: hub→leaf (direct children only, via `:scope >` so it doesn't double-count leaves nested inside a group), hub→group-header, and group-header→its own tasks only while that specific group is expanded.

  Verified with real screenshots and direct property checks, not assumed: a real screenshot confirmed 7 sub-branches formed and the line count dropped from 165 to 143 (29 items consolidated into 7 group headers, math checks out: 165 − 29 + 7 = 143); expanding one group added exactly 4 lines back (143→147) matching its exact item count; a task clicked *inside* an expanded sub-branch still opened the correct detail panel; `getBoundingClientRect()` confirmed the root sits fully within the viewport (`x>=0 && right<=innerWidth`) rather than just eyeballing centered-looking output. One false alarm caught and correctly dismissed rather than chased: an early screenshot appeared to show a stale collapse-arrow after expanding a group, but a clean, isolated re-check of that exact same interaction (chevron text + collapsed class both read directly from the DOM) confirmed the underlying logic was already correct — a Playwright screenshot-timing artifact, not a real bug, verified by not trusting the first signal alone.

  **Fifth follow-up, same session, direct request**: *"Make the whole page nonscrollable while making it possible to scroll within the page. Also make it so that we can minimize the small windows."* Two changes:

  1. **Page-level scroll lock** — `overflow:hidden` was already on `body`, but not on `html`; added it there too rather than relying on Chromium's own body→viewport overflow propagation, removing the ambiguity outright. The panels that already needed internal scrolling (`.legend`'s `overflow-y:auto`, `.flat-view`'s `overflow:auto`, `.detail-panel`'s `overflow-y:auto`) were untouched — they keep scrolling inside their own box exactly as before, only the outer page itself is now pinned.

  2. **Minimize the Altimeter and Legend panels** — one shared `.panel-min` button + `.panel-min-label` markup added to both, one shared click handler (`querySelectorAll(".panel-min")`) rather than a bespoke handler per panel, so any future `.panel` gets minimize support for free by reusing the same markup. Minimizing hides every real child (`.panel.minimized > *:not(.panel-min):not(.panel-min-label){ display:none; }`) and shrinks the panel down to a small pill showing just its label; clicking the pill itself (not only the button) restores it.

  Verified live, not assumed: `document.documentElement.scrollHeight > clientHeight` is `false`, `window.scrollTo(0,500)` leaves `window.scrollY` at `0`; clicking each panel's minimize button flips its class and drops the label's computed `display` to `none`, clicking the collapsed pill restores it (both directions checked via `getComputedStyle`/class reads, not just visually); switching to the 2D view afterward confirmed `#flatView` still scrolls internally (`scrollTop` settable to 200) while `window.scrollY` stays `0` throughout — the two behaviors coexist as intended. Screenshots of both minimized states confirm the pills render cleanly with no leftover child content peeking through.

## Scopes & North Star

*Reference — the thesis-completion criteria and the generalization vision. The
actionable work lives in the [Task List](#task-list) (Scope #1/#2/#3 + the 8
capability dimensions); this is the* why.

### The three scopes *(thesis = all three)*
Chosen to span the interesting space — *data entry*, *cross-app transfer*,
*conditional judgment* — so the claim is "clones varied GUI workflows," not "fills
one form."

1. **Data Entry Form Filling** — *in progress ([Scope #1](#task-list)).* Single-app
   key-value entry, (mostly) linear. Loop proven on the Policy section (clones
   order, fills, submits); remaining = multi-tab, multi-record, cold-start.
   Perception = UIA.
2. **Web Form → Excel** — *not started ([Scope #2](#task-list)).* Cross-application
   transfer (web source → Excel grid); 2D target; mixed perception. Excel
   perception swap **PROVEN** (`ExcelObserver` normalizes to canonical); remaining
   = web source, action on cells, demos, train.
3. **Email / Ticket Triage** — *working on mock data ([Scope #3](#task-list)),
   Inbox Router approach; real Gmail deliberately deferred (2026-09-02).*
   Based on a passively-learned per-sender pattern profile
   (Gmail API + OAuth2, no manual labeling), an incoming email is replied to
   (fast-fill matcher, or a scoped LLM-auto-draft-and-send exception in the
   demo script), forwarded, scheduled (content extracted to a plain log plus
   a real Google Calendar event as of 2026-08-30/31, no reuse model needed),
   sent as a cold email (task-list-driven, real, LLM-generated and sent),
   flagged
   for a person, or left alone — then the outcome is confirmed by reply.
   Redefined (2026-08-29) to the tasks a virtual assistant actually does on
   email, dropping the earlier Route-to-Scope-#1/#2 model entirely — Scope #3
   no longer connects to Scope #1/#2 at all. Chosen approach (2026-08-16)
   doesn't need branching ([Big Three #3](#task-list)) at all, unlike the
   originally-planned GUI-demonstration path — decisions come from Gmail API
   classification, not a cloned UI action loop. Kept to decisions inferable
   from *visible* content (avoid hidden-intent, Fundamental roadblock A).

### North Star — generalization (beyond the thesis)
The thesis is *bounded* to those three, but the **architecture is built to
generalize** — that is the real goal. The novel contribution: a **personalized,
demonstration-learned GUI agent** that learns how *this user* does a task and
reproduces *their* workflow — which scripted RPA (no learning) and generic
computer-use agents (not personalized) don't.

Already partly real: perception is an **adapter** (UIA + Excel today, one shared
schema) and the `transformer(WHERE) + LLM(WHAT)` loop is perception-agnostic. The
path beyond the thesis — a vision perception adapter
([Big Three #1](#task-list)) + LLM-induced control-flow
([Big Three #3](#task-list)) — turns "three GUI scopes" into "any GUI
workflow learned from demonstration."

---

## Questions, Concerns, and Concepts

Open threads, honest unknowns/risks, and the **design concepts** (architectural
decisions + approaches like the WHERE/HOW division and DAgger) that shape how the
system evolves. **Questions** = unknowns to resolve · **Concerns** = risks to watch
· **Concepts** = decisions made + design patterns to build toward.

### Decisions & concepts

**WHAT does the transformer learn — Pure (A) vs Division-of-labor (B)? → CHOSE B.**
The 3-tab navigation marathon (2026-06-11) exposed that the transformer's
*action-type head* (deciding click-vs-type per step) is **unstable** — it whipsaws
between all-click (combobox/field spiral) and all-keyboard (tabs through, never
clicks) across retrains. Two ways forward:

- **Option A — Pure transformer.** The transformer learns *everything*, including
  click-vs-type, purely from demos, **zero rules.** Honest cost: a days-to-weeks
  data + training grind (more consistent + transition-dense demos, bigger model,
  stable recipe, likely DAgger), with real risk of a BC ceiling (long-horizon +
  rare events + compounding error is the textbook case where pure BC underperforms).
- **Option B — Division of labor *(CHOSEN)*.** The **transformer drives WHERE** —
  which element, what order, *when to switch tabs* (the learned, personalized
  navigation = the thesis claim, 100% intact). **Widget TYPE drives HOW** — type
  into an editcontrol, click a tabitem, open+select a combobox, toggle a checkbox
  (universal GUI mechanics, identical for every user/app — *not* form-specific).

**Why B:** click-vs-type is **not personalization** — it's universal plumbing, true
for every app. Forcing the unstable head to learn it blocks scope #1 for zero
thesis benefit. B keeps the personalization claim ("learns the user's navigation/
order/workflow") fully intact while freeing the one unstable decision onto rules
that **already exist in the agent** (combobox/checkbox handlers etc. are already
widget heuristics — the agent was never "pure"). Net: ~5 universal widget→action
rules, no form-specifics, navigation still 100% learned. Finishes scope #1 in
hours vs weeks. *(If a reviewer asks: the claim is "clones the user's workflow,"
not "learned how to physically touch every widget with zero rules.")*

### DAgger — how to implement *(if/when pure-BC drift needs it)*
The principled fix for the live drift seen on 2026-06-11 (agent leaves the form,
oscillates) = train on the states the agent ITSELF visits, not just clean demos.
Loop: train → run policy → label the correct action for visited (drift) states →
aggregate → retrain → repeat. Labeling is the only real choice:
- **Manual (closest to ready):** the `CorrectionHandler` seam already exists but
  **captures 0 steps (broken)** — fix it so after a failed/no-change step the agent
  pauses, the user performs the right action, and `(state, action)` is recorded.
  Targeted (only fix mistakes), far cheaper than re-recording passes.
- **Autonomous (no human):** demo-retrieval oracle — embed the visited state
  (`encode_state`), find the nearest demo state, copy its action as the label.
  Approximate but fully automatic; fits the "learn from demos, not manual" goal.
- **Blocker:** `CorrectionHandler` capturing 0 is *why DAgger isn't usable yet* —
  un-breaking it is the first concrete step. Needs a few run→correct→retrain rounds
  to converge.

### Open technical questions
- **Loss-weighting is less plug-and-play — how do we improve it in the future?**
  The 2026-06-12 rare-event fix (up-weight the rare action's frames in the loss so
  the model learns it without distorting the data — see Concepts) still requires
  *manually labelling which action class is rare* (here: clicks whose target is a
  `tabitem`). For a truly general system, that labelling should be **automatic.**
  Directions to explore:
  - **Auto-detect rare classes by frequency** — at dataset build, histogram the
    target action classes (per element-type / per action), then weight each
    **inversely to its frequency** (`w ∝ 1/freq`). Zero manual tagging; any rare
    action self-emphasizes. The general version of what we hand-built for tabs.
  - **Focal loss** — automatically down-weights easy/common examples and focuses
    gradient on hard/rare ones, *without* any class labels at all. The most
    plug-and-play; no frequency bookkeeping.
  - **Make rare actions CONDITIONAL (state feature)** — give the model a signal
    that predicts the rare action (e.g. "section-complete fraction"), so rarity
    stops mattering. More robust but needs the trigger identified per action.
  → Likely end-state: **inverse-frequency auto-weighting (or focal loss) as the
  universal default**, with optional state-features for robustness where a clear
  trigger exists. Reusable across every scope's rare actions.
- **Will full-form cloning scale on data alone?** Policy tab (~10 fields) needed
  ~20–30 passes. The full form is 176 fields / 8 tabs with rare **tab-transition**
  events. Unknown whether ~30–50 passes clone it, or whether long-trajectory BC
  needs far more demos than is feasible to hand-record. *(The `three_Tabs` probe
  is the cheap test of this.)*
- **Cold-start without hardcoding.** First click from a blank screen is ~0% and
  proven resistant to data + model size. Needs DAgger / a learned start-signal —
  but a deterministic anchor is off the table (no hardcoding). Open: how?
- **Is the >95% transformer-dependency target even right?** Stuck ~62–68%. By
  design the LLM owns *values*, so the transformer can't drive the value-typing
  steps. Maybe the honest ceiling is lower and the metric should exclude
  value-steps — needs a definition decision.
- **Excel action half is untested.** Perception swap is proven, but *acting* on
  cells (click → type → Enter, grid navigation) isn't. Will the form-specific
  executor handlers (combobox/checkbox) interfere on Excel? Probe before P1.
- **Cycle-restart loop root cause** — new record re-clicks the already-checked
  Renewal checkbox → Tab → no-change loop. Is it a per-record state-reset bug or a
  model issue? Decides whether it's an easy fix or needs data.

### Strategic / thesis concerns
- **Is perception-swap enough evidence, or must we show full Excel completion?**
  We proved Excel *perception* conforms (zero edits). The stronger claim — a model
  actually completing a task on a 2nd app — needs action + demos + training. How
  much does the thesis require?
- **Scope #3 (triage) is no longer stalled.** The originally-planned
  GUI-demonstration path is still genuinely blocked on Action-Space (#2) and
  Control-Flow (#3) — those remain parked in North Star. But the chosen
  Inbox Router approach (Gmail API + OAuth2, see Task List) doesn't depend
  on either one, so Scope #3 is actively moving without waiting for them.
- **Web perception may force the vision adapter early.** Scope #2's web source may
  lack a clean accessibility tree → could pull Big Three #1 (vision) forward of
  plan. De-risk: probe the web source's tree before committing.

### Risks / debt
- **Slow iteration loop** — every model test = record → retrain → live run.
  Velocity risk for the P0 reliability work.
- **Is the DAgger / correction loop actually working?** `CorrectionHandler`
  captures *0 steps* in live runs. Either nothing to correct, or it's not wired —
  verify before relying on it for cold-start.
- **Stale tests** — `test_transformer_bc / html_detector / two_state` fail to
  collect (`ModuleNotFound: components.intelligence.model.dataset`). Fix or delete.
- **Pixel-coordinate brittleness** — clicks use absolute screen px from the
  observer; sensitive to resolution / window position. Robustness concern at scale.

---

## Finished Tasks

Completed work, preserved for reference.

### Navigation cloning (2026-06)
- **Proved the transformer clones the demonstrated order** — top-down (74%) vs
  bottom-up (93%), same architecture → not a bias, genuine cloning.
- **`is_filled` perception feature** — model can see which fields are done →
  stopped the end-game looping.
- **Action-space collapse → {click, type}** — action-type accuracy 50% → 80%,
  click accuracy up for free.
- **Wired action-type into the agent** — transformer decides click vs type (was a
  hardcoded "fillable & empty" rule).
- **Recorder combobox fix** — clicks while a dropdown is open are value-selections
  (land on the field under the dropdown) → dropped at record time. Killed the
  phantom "Expiration Date" pollution.
- **Tail-oversampling** — emphasized the `… → Submit` finish → model learned to
  submit on its own (no hardcoded completion rule).
- **End-to-end run** — fills the whole form in the learned order + clicks Submit +
  advances record, transformer-driven, no crutch, no human.
- **Recorder focus fix** — clicks always get a fresh snapshot (was reusing stale
  state during bursts → focus stuck).
- **`scripts/clean_demos.py`, `test_clone.py`, `oversample_tails.py`,
  `replicate.py`** added.

### Intelligence & Training
- **GPU training** — CUDA 12.4 / PyTorch 2.6.0+cu124, RTX 4050.
- **Best-acc checkpoint** — saves on `val_acc + click_acc`, not val_loss.
- **Dataset init cache** — `.dataset_cache.pkl`, retrain init ~1 sec.
- **LayerNorm on pointer heads** — fixed bilinear Q×K divergence (197M loss bug).
- **Data augmentation** — `augment_traces.py` (bbox/click/confidence jitter).
- **Tasks/ reorganization** — model.pt, ruleset.md under `tasks/form_filling/`.

### Agent & Merge Logic
- **Crutch-gating in pure mode** — VISITED-ADVANCE + LLM-takeover-when-weak gated
  off under `disable_auto_handlers` (they fought the model).
- **Fix LLM click position** — `_merge` resolves LLM target by label; transformer
  click is fallback.

### Ruleset & Spec System
- **Correctional ruleset** — `RuleExtractor.correct()` + auto-call on record end.
- **Spec injection** — `ruleset.md` appended to LLM system prompt.

### Evaluation
- **Per-run metrics** — `eval_metrics.evaluate_run` (TCR, action/value accuracy).
- **BC fidelity scorer** — `bc_fidelity.py` vs gold standard, trend in
  `bc_progress.jsonl`.

### Infrastructure
- **Capsule registry** — per-task model routing.
- **`.gitignore`** — `data/demos/`, traces, caches, model binaries excluded.
